6.9 Ethical Standards Board (ESB) 


The Ethical Standards Board of ICAI is the designated standard-setting authority for ethical governance 
within the profession. The Board develops and issues ethical standards and other pronouncements for 
Chartered Accountants, and works towards evolving a dynamic and contemporary Code of Ethics that 
retains the long-cherished ideals of excellence, independence, and integrity, while protecting the dignity 
and interests of members. The Board takes measures to ensure the independence of auditors, assists 
in the adoption and implementation of ethical standards by members in practice and in service, and 
promotes public awareness of the integrity, objectivity, competence, and professionalism of members. 


The CA Connect Portal, launched by the Ethical Standards Board in 202i and redesigned in 2024, 
continues to provide an effective listing platform that serves as an essential bridge between 
clients/service receivers and Chartered Accountants. 


Significant Achievements: 


e Global Ethics Day was observed on i5th October 2025 under the theme '#EthicsRe-envisioned'’, 
with a webinar organised on 'Contemporary Developments in Professional Ethics'. An Ethics 
Fortnight was also celebrated from 8th to 23rd October 2025, featuring knowledge-sharing 
programmes organised by branches under the aegis of the Board, including the publication of 
articles in newspapers and periodicals and enrolment of members on the CA Connect Portal. 

e The special edition of the CA Journal for October 2025 was published on the theme ‘Internal 
Audit and Ethics’. 

e A Quiz on Ethical Issues was conducted for members from ist to I5th October 2025, encouraging 
engagement and awareness on ethical matters. 

e The revised i3th edition of the Code of Ethics is being released. 

e The Board continued to issue the monthly awareness column 'Know Your Ethics' in the ICAI 
Journal and maintained an active presence on X (Twitter), sharing creatives on important topics 
to promote optimum adoption of the Code of Ethics. 

e The Board clarified various ethical issues raised by members and stakeholders relating to auditor 
independence, permissible professional services, conflict of interest, engagement in other 
business/occupation, holding of other designations/positions, and advertisement and solicitation. 
A clarification was issued regarding the printing of photographs of members on visiting cards, in 
response to a query in the 'FAQs on Ethical Issues, 2025 edition’ publication. In addition, more 
than 300 queries from members on professional ethical issues were addressed and resolved by 
the Secretariat. 

e The Board provided inputs to IFAC’s Member Engagement: Adoption & Implementation 
Questionnaire, 2025, on 7th May 2025, and contributed inputs for the IESBA-IAASB Joint 
Standard-Setters (JSS) Meeting held on 7th-9th May 2025 on jurisdictional developments of 
international relevance. 


Seminars, Programmes and Webinars on the Code of Ethics 


The Ethical Standards Board organised approximately 3 seminars, programmes, and webinars during 
the year to create awareness on the provisions of the Code of Ethics and ethical issues among members. 
These were conducted across locations including Bilaspur, Hyderabad, Chennai, Tirupur, Kerala, 
Kottayam, Faridabad, Cuttack, Jaipur, Amritsar, Agra, Sonepat, Prayagraj, Rajasthan, Palakkad, Bihar, 
Jodhpur, Bhilai, Satara, and Kolhapur, covering topics ranging from the Code of Ethics, professional 
misconduct, disciplinary mechanisms, NOCLAR, audit documentation, and advertisement and 
solicitation, to professional skepticism in tax audit. Ten dedicated webinars were also held on themes 
such as significant ethical expectations, disciplinary procedures, advertisement and solicitation, and the 
exposure draft of the Code of Ethics.

6.0 DIGITAL ACCOUNTING AND ASSURANCE BOARD (DAAB) 


The Digital Accounting and Assurance Board (DAAB) of ICAI continued to strengthen ICAI’s leadership 
in the areas of Information Systems Audit, Forensic Accounting, Cybersecurity, Data Protection, Digital 
Forensics and Technology-driven Assurance during the year 2025-26. The Board focused on 
professional capacity building, the development of standards, publications, practical training 
infrastructure and stakeholder engagement in emerging technology domains. 


(I) Standard-Setting in Emerging Technology Domains 


A significant milestone during the year was the issuance of the Information Systems Audit Standards 
(ISAS), making ICAI the first professional accounting body globally to release dedicated standards in 
the domain of Information Systems Audit. During the year, the Board also updated the Forensic 
Accounting and Investigation Standards (FAIS), along with the Implementation Guide, to align them 
with global best practices, emerging technologies and international adoption requirements. The Board 
further undertook global outreach initiatives for FAIS, which are increasingly being recognised by 
regulators, enforcement agencies and institutions in India and abroad. 


(II) Professional Education and Skilling 
The Board undertook major initiatives in professional education and skilling: 


¢ DISA 4.0: The Board initiated the upgradation of the DISA Course to DISA 4.0, with an enhanced 
focus on practical learning, cybersecurity, digital governance and hands-on audit tools. 

e FAFD to Diploma in Forensic Investigation (DFI): The Certificate Course on Forensic 
Accounting and Fraud Detection (FAFD) continued successfully, while work progressed towards 
its conversion into the Diploma in Forensic Investigation (DFI). 

¢ Data Protection Compliance & Audit Certification (DPCAC): During the year, the Board 
launched the DPCAC programme, aligned with the Digital Personal Data Protection framework. 

e Course on Cybersecurity & Audit Skilling: The specialised course was approved by the 
Council and shall be launched shortly. 

e¢ Executive Development Programmes: Programmes on Digital Forensics, Data Analytics and 
Power BI were also conducted. 


(III) Practical Learning Infrastructure 


The Board strengthened practical learning infrastructure through the initiation of a phased upgradation 
of Data Analytics/Forensic Labs at ICAI Centres of Excellence, beginning with Hyderabad as a pilot 
project. 


(IV) Conferences, Outreach and Capacity Building 


The DAAB organised several national-level conferences, seminars, outreach programmes and webinars 
during the year. Major events included the Digital Transformation Finance Summit (DxFS) 2025 at 
Hyderabad and Future Proof Forensics 2025 at Mumbai, which witnessed participation from regulators, 
banks, enforcement agencies, industry leaders and technology experts. The Board also conducted 
specialised capacity-building programmes for organisations such as the C&AG, NIA, SEBI, ICLS 
Academy and CID Telangana.

(V) Technical Publications 


The Board released and updated various technical publications and guidance materials in emerging 
areas, including Cloud Computing, Blockchain, Internet of Things (IoT) and the ABCD of Technology. 
These initiatives have contributed significantly towards strengthening digital trust, technology 
assurance and professional excellence within the accounting and financial ecosystem.

6.4 EXPERT ADVISORY COMMITTEE (EAC) 


In a dynamic economic landscape marked by increasingly complex transactions and the continuous 
evolution of financial reporting requirements, accounting professionals are often required to address 
intricate and emerging issues. Such situations demand professional judgement supported by well- 
reasoned and authoritative guidance to maintain the credibility and quality of financial reporting. The 
Expert Advisory Committee (EAC) serves as a key technical body in this regard by providing independent 
and well-deliberated opinions on intricate accounting matters. Over the years, the Committee has 
established itself as a valuable and trusted source of technical guidance, assisting members across 
industries, as well as government and regulatory institutions, in addressing practical accounting 
challenges. 


(I) Expert Opinions 


The Committee responds to queries from members strictly in line with the Advisory Service Rules. Its 
scope is confined to issues relating to accounting and auditing principles; accordingly, issues involving 
the legal interpretation of statutory enactments and/or cases of professional misconduct are not 
answered by it. Further, as per the Rules, matters pending before the Board of Discipline or the 
Disciplinary Committee of the Institute, or before an appropriate department of the Government or the 
Income-tax authorities, are not answered by the Committee. The Advisory Service Rules are accessible 


on the ICAI website(https://www.icai.org/post/495) 


The opinions issued by the Expert Advisory Committee are based on the specific facts and circumstances 
provided by the querist, taking into account the relevant laws, statutes and applicable accounting and 
auditing principles. Each opinion clearly states the date of its finalisation and should be read considering 
the developments and/or amendments to laws or standards that may have occurred after that date. 


(II) Opinions Finalised 


During the period from * April, 2025 to 30 April, 2026, the Committee finalised forty-three (43) 
opinions on a wide range of queries raised by members involving issues related to accounting principles. 


[भाग ता---खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 229 


(III) Information Technology (IT) Initiatives 


Opinions issued by the Committee are made available through an online Search Application on the ICAI 
website, enabling easy access. The EAC has upgraded its Search Application with more refined search 
features, enabling next-level search to deliver more precise results and provide members with a 
seamless and_ efficient research experience. The salient features of the Application 


(https://eacopinion.icai.org/ ) are: 


e An extensive repository of around i,700 opinions, empowering users to explore the Opinions 
issued by the EAC so far. 

e Search facility based on keyword(s), date of opinion, volume of the Compendium of Opinions, 
etc. 

e A ‘Recent Opinions’ tab containing the latest opinions issued by the Committee, updated regularly 
to provide up-to-date information to members. 

e An advanced and upgraded search facility that enables users to locate EAC opinions using 
keywords, combinations of keywords and/or the date or period of issuance, with enhanced 
features delivering more precise results and significantly improving ease of access and usability. 


(IV) Dissemination of Knowledge 


¢ Webinars: As part of its outreach initiatives, the Committee organised a webinar on the theme 
“Recent EAC Opinions under Ind AS Framework” on i6 December, 2025, with a view to 
disseminating knowledge and creating awareness amongst members and other stakeholders. 

e Publishing of Opinions in the Journal: Selected opinions of general relevance are also 
published in the Institute’s journal, ‘The Chartered Accountant’. 


(V) Publications — Compendium of Opinions 


In order to make the technical guidance contained in the Opinions available to accounting professionals 
and other stakeholders, the Committee periodically compiles and publishes its opinions as the 
Compendium of Opinions. To date, forty-four (44) volumes have been released, serving as a valuable 
reference resource for professionals. The Compendium of Opinions is accessible on the ICAI website 
(https://www. icai.org/post/icai-publications-expert-advisory-committee ) . 


6.5 CORPORATE LAW COMMITTEE (CLC) 


The Corporate Law Committee (CLC) (Earlier named as Corporate Laws and Corporate Governance 
Committee (CL&CGC) works towards strengthening the corporate regulatory framework through policy 
advocacy, stakeholder engagement and collaboration with the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA). The 
Committee also promotes knowledge enhancement and professional excellence in the areas of 
corporate laws and governance. 


The key highlights of the year are as under: 
(I) Policy Contributions and Recommendations 


e Submitted comprehensive recommendations for the operationalisation of Section 29A of the 
Companies Act, 20i3 (periodic financial reporting by specified unlisted companies), covering 
proportionate thresholds, half-yearly reporting aligned with SEBI practices, mandatory Board 
approval, practical timelines, flexibility between audit and limited review, and simplified e-forms 
to reduce the compliance burden. 

e Submitted detailed suggestions on SEBI’s Consultation Paper on Appointment of Auditors and 
Related Party Transactions of listed entities, aimed at strengthening governance and 
transparency while ensuring operational feasibility, ease of compliance and balanced regulatory 
implementation. 

e Submitted a representation seeking exemption from dematerialisation for Section 8 companies, 
considering their non-profit nature and limited shareholder activity, and provided technical inputs 


230 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SEc.4] 


to SEBI on secretarial compliance, auditor appointments and related party transactions to ensure 
a balance between regulatory strength and practical implementation. 

e Supported the Prime Minister Internship Scheme (PMIS) by facilitating the placement of trained 
interns to enhance youth employability and recommended treating vocational training as CSR 
expenditure and post-employment costs as normal business expenses, in line with Section 35 
and Schedule VII of the Companies Act, 2043. 


(II) Regulatory and Governance Advisory 


e Examined requests from CPSEs under the Ministry of Heavy Industries for relaxation in strike-off 
provisions involving unresolved liabilities and legal proceedings, and recommended a balanced 
approach ensuring administrative efficiency while safeguarding stakeholder interests and 
upholding governance and accountability standards. 

e Reviewed the representation from Tata Power Renewable Energy Limited (TPREL) seeking 
exemption from appointing Independent Directors in certain captive SPVs, and advised against 
such exemption, citing inconsistency with sound corporate governance and potential dilution of 
the intent of Section 49 of the Companies Act, 203. 


(III) Support to Government Initiatives 


e Collaborated with the MCA on the simplification of corporate laws through nationwide stakeholder 
consultations, and contributed to the rollout of MCA2i Version 3 through technical support, 
grievance redressal and facilitation, while initiating consultations for MCA2 Version 4. 

e Continued engagement through meetings with MCA officials, participation in user acceptance 
testing and joint outreach programmes, along with awareness initiatives, webinars and training 
sessions to support compliance readiness, especially for filings such as AOC-4 and MGT-7. 


(IV) Capacity Building and Outreach Programmes 


e Undertook national outreach initiatives, including participation in “Vision Rajasthan 2026” at 
Sirohi, Rajasthan, and promotion of LLP adoption in under-penetrated regions, along with 
capacity building through conferences, conclaves and certificate courses on CSR Reporting. 

e¢ Conducted extensive training programmes, seminars, webinars and outreach initiatives to 
enhance professional competency, strengthen compliance awareness and promote good 
governance practices across the country.

6.7 COOPERATION AND NPO COMMITTEE 


The cooperative sector, comprising more than 8.5 lakh cooperatives across the country, plays a pivotal 
role in India’s socio-economic development by promoting inclusive and sustainable growth. Recognising 
the significance of this sector, the Government of India established the dedicated Ministry of 
Cooperation in 202i with the vision of achieving the mission of “Sahkar Se Samriddhi / सहकार a समृद्धि”. 
In alignment with this national vision, ICAI has constituted the Cooperation and NPO Committee (Earlier 
part of Committee on Commercial Laws and Economic Advisory & NPO Cooperative) to contribute 
towards the strengthening and development of the cooperative and NPO sectors in the country. 


The Committee engaged with key policy-making and regulatory authorities for the holistic development 
of the cooperative sector. In this regard, the Committee closely coordinated with the Central Registrar 
of Cooperative Societies in the drafting of the Multi-State Co-operative (Auditor’s Report) Order 
(MSCARO), 2026. 


Further, the Committee coordinated with the Ministry of Cooperation, the National Council for 
Cooperative Training (NCCT) and Tribhuvan Sahkari University (TSU) towards implementing 
transformational reforms in the cooperative sector. Additionally, the Committee coordinated with the 
Cooperative Departments of various States and Union Territories to strengthen capacity-building 
initiatives for stakeholders in the cooperative ecosystem across the country. 


6.8 CENTRE FOR AUDIT QUALITY COMMITTEE (CAQC) 


The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India established the Centre for Audit Quality Directorate 
(CAQD) in 2020 to focus on enhancing the quality of audit practices in India. In 2026, the Directorate 
was reconstituted as the Centre for Audit Quality Committee (CAQC), strengthening its role and 
governance framework. The CAQC primarily focuses on the qualitative aspects of the audit function, 
ensuring continuous improvement in audit quality and contributing to enhancing the global standing of 
Indian audit firms. 


In furtherance of its mandate, the Committee undertakes a broad spectrum of initiatives aimed at 
strengthening audit and assurance quality. These include developing a framework for audit and 
assurance quality and formulating the Audit Quality Maturity Model (AQMM) based on Audit Quality 


[भाग [-खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 


233 


Indicators; conducting certificate courses and awareness programmes to enable members to perform 
audits in compliance with auditing principles; developing publications and guidance materials to assist 
members in improving their professional practices and establishing robust systems and processes; and 
facilitating the development and adoption of technological resources for efficient audit practices, 
besides undertaking functions assigned by the Council or the President from time to time. The activities 
undertaken during the period under report are as under: 


(I) Utilities Developed and Updated by CAQC 


The CAQC develops and provides technological resources, tools and utilities to facilitate the smooth 
conduct of quality audits and assurance services, while supporting audit firms in enhancing audit quality, 
operational efficiency and regulatory compliance. During the year, the following utilities were 
developed/updated: 


e Checklist for Certificates issued by Chartered Accountants: The CAQC developed a 
Checklist for Certificates issued by Chartered Accountants, in alignment with the Guidance Note 
on Reports or Certificates for Special Purposes (Revised 206). It is an easy-to-understand and 
easy-to-implement tool that assists members in ensuring compliance with the various aspects of 
the Guidance Note while issuing certificates. 


e Utility for Bank Branch Audit — 2026 Edition (updated): To help members in the smooth 
conduct of bank branch audits, the CAQC has developed an Excel utility based on the Guidance 
Note on Audit of Banks and the Technical Guide on Long Form Audit Report issued by the AASB. 
The Utility has been updated, incorporating the changes in the Guidance Note on Audit of Banks 
(2026 Edition). 


e Utility for Review and Monitoring Tool on Standards on Auditing Compliance 
(updated): 


The tool provides comprehensive checklists for ensuring compliance with the Standards on 
Auditing, tracks audit progress via a work status dashboard and documents team activities, 
including the time spent on each audit. The tool has been updated with the addition of a ‘Whether 
Applicable’ column in the Dashboard, indicating the applicability of each Standard on Auditing 
(SA); if a particular SA is not applicable, the user can select ‘No’, upon which the corresponding 
SA sheet displays a ‘No’ status and restricts any further inputs. 


e Utility for Determining Materiality (updated): The utility assists in calculating materiality 
thresholds for statutory audits, categorises risk assessment (low/medium/high) and aids in 
forming audit opinions based on material misstatements. It has been enhanced by adding a 
column ‘Reason for choosing the level of risk’ in the materiality calculation sheet, enabling users 
to document their rationale for selecting the appropriate level of risk. 


In previous years, the Committee had successfully introduced the following utilities: 


e Utility for Self-evaluation under AQMM v 2.0: The utility assists firms in self-evaluating their 
maturity level on the basis of the Audit Quality Indicators defined under AQMM v 2.0, enabling 
them to identify areas where competencies are strong or lacking and to develop a roadmap for 
achieving a higher level of audit maturity. 


e Utility on Engagement Letters: The utility has been designed to simplify and streamline the 
process of generating engagement letters for assurance clients, enabling auditors and audit firms 
to generate multiple engagement letters with a single click, saving time and eliminating the 
complexities involved in their preparation.

Accountants, and is aligned with ICAI’s Guidance Note on Reports and Certificates for Special 
Purposes (Revised 2046). 

e Bridging the Expectation Gap — Audit vs. Forensic: The publication explains the 
fundamental differences between an audit and a forensic engagement — two professional 
activities that are often perceived as similar but serve distinctly different purposes. 


7. PROFESSIONAL DEVELOPMENT COMMITTEES 
7.i AI COMMITTEE 


The AI Committee (constituted as the “AI in ICAI” Committee in the Council Year 2024-25) spearheads 
the integration of Artificial Intelligence across the profession and the Institute’s operations, enhancing 
services for members, students and employees. The Committee focuses on building AI capability 
through structured certifications and continuous learning, developing Al-powered tools and platforms, 
creating knowledge infrastructure, fostering innovation and forging strategic collaborations with the 
Government, industry and global technology leaders. The key initiatives undertaken during the period 
under report are outlined below: 


236 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SEc.4] 


(I) Capacity Building and Learning Initiatives 


e AICA-— AI Certificate Course for Chartered Accountants: A flagship structured certification 
programme comprising Level i, a three-day physical programme covering foundational AI 
concepts and tools, and Level 2, a five-day blended-format programme covering advanced 
applications with practical use cases. Over 750 batches have been conducted and 42,000+ 
members trained at Level i, while 40+ batches have been conducted and ,500+ members 
trained at Level 2. 

e AI Refresher Series: Weekly virtual sessions on Level i, launched in April 2025, ensure 
continuous learning and updates on emerging AI trends and tools; the Level 2 series commenced 
in April 2026. Both series are free of cost for eligible members. 

e Faculty Identification Programme (FIP) on AI: A structured initiative to build a strong pool 
of Al-trained faculty across India for scaling learning initiatives; 6 FIPs have been conducted till 
date, with the 7" scheduled on 97 -0" May, 2026.

e ICAI CA Student GPT: A dedicated AI assistant tailored for students to support learning and 
examination preparation.

document and analytical capabilities for professional use. 

e Industry Forum GPT: An AI tool built on the data of 5,000+ listed companies, enabling industry 
benchmarking and analysis.

7.2 MEMBERS IN INDUSTRY & BUSINESS COMMITTEE 


Members in Industry & Business Committee of ICAI (Earlier named as Committee For Members in 
Industry and Business) continues to drive professional excellence through a diverse portfolio of strategic 
initiatives. The key activities of the Committee during the period under report are as under: 


(I) Campus Placement Programmes 


A cornerstone of the Committee’s efforts is the Campus Placement Programme, which facilitated career 
transitions for thousands of newly qualified CAs in 2025-26. During the 6ist edition (February—March 
2025), 3,729 offers were made, with a top salary of 29.00 lakh per annum. This momentum was 
sustained in the 62™ edition (August-September 2025), which saw 3,785 employment offers and a 
highest CTC of %26.60 lakh per annum. Preparations for the 63 Campus Placement Programme 
(February-March 2026) were geared towards qualifying candidates from the November 2025 
examinations, with the registration process commencing in January 2026 to continue this career 
trajectory. 


To ensure candidates are “corporate-ready”, the Committee also conducted intensive i-2-day 
Orientation Programmes focusing on interview techniques and soft skills for the 0,653 candidates 
registered in the latter session. 


(II) Professional Networking and Continuous Learning 


Beyond initial placements, the Committee fosters lifelong learning and professional networking through 
dedicated forums and continuous education modules. Major initiatives include the CFO and CHRO 
Forums, which serve as high-level networking platforms for senior professionals. The “Growth Gears” 
talk-show series and various industry-specific webinars have been instrumental in providing members 
with contemporary insights into the start-up ecosystem, real estate and digital transformation. 


238 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SEc.4] 


Furthermore, the Committee maintains an active schedule of Seminars, Workshops and Residential 
Refresher Courses (RRCs) to help members meet their Continuing Professional Education (CPE) 
requirements while staying updated on global business trends. 


(III) Recognition Programmes and Leadership Development 


The Committee’s commitment to excellence is also reflected in its recognition programmes and targeted 
development for high achievers. These include the ICAI Awards for CA achievers and the specialised 
“40 Under 40 - CA Business Leader Award”. For academic standouts, the Management Development 
Programmes (MDPs) for rank holders provide advanced leadership training in collaboration with premier 
business schools. Collectively, these initiatives ensure that the CMI&B remains a vital link between the 
accounting profession and the evolving needs of global industry. 


7.3 AGGREGATION OF CA FIRMS COMMITTEE (ACAFC) 


The Aggregation of CA Firms Committee (ACAFC), constituted in 2024-25, is driving a transformational 
journey from “Ease to Excellence in Practice” for CA firms in India. With a clear strategic mandate, the 
ACAFC is undertaking a comprehensive review and revision of existing guidelines to ensure they are 
relevant, adaptive and aligned with the evolving professional and regulatory landscape. Through its 
initiatives, the ACAFC empowers CA firms to expand expertise, scale operations, embrace digital 
solutions and navigate the dynamic professional ecosystem with agility. By streamlining processes, 
reducing procedural barriers and fostering a culture of resilience and excellence, the Committee 
cultivates an environment where firms can thrive. Leveraging stakeholder engagement, structured 
capacity building and continuous feedback, the ACAFC ensures that CA firms are not only equipped to 
meet domestic challenges but are also poised to compete and collaborate effectively in an increasingly 
global market. 


Key Initiatives of ACAFC 


e FAQs and the Model Merger Deed on the ICAI (Merger & Demerger of CA Firms) 
Guidelines, 2024: This publication comprises a comprehensive set of Frequently Asked 
Questions (FAQs) designed to address commonly encountered issues and provide practical, 
actionable guidance to members. The FAQs serve as an important reference tool, offering clarity 
on interpretational and procedural aspects to enable smooth and efficient adoption of the revised 
Guidelines. To further support firms in operationalising mergers, a Model Merger Deed has been 
annexed, outlining essential provisions including the terms of merger, governance and 
management structure, dispute resolution mechanisms, client transition processes and 
confidentiality safeguards, thereby facilitating a seamless and legally compliant merger process 
for CA firms. 

e FAQs on the ICAI (Aggregation of LLPs) Guidelines, 2024: To assist stakeholders in 
understanding and implementing the Aggregation framework for LLPs, the ACAFC developed a 
detailed set of FAQs addressing key aspects of the Guidelines. These FAQs provide clarity on 
eligibility criteria, procedural requirements, compliance obligations and practical implementation 
considerations, enabling members to navigate the Aggregation of LLPs framework with 
confidence. 

e CA-Setu — Networking Portal for CAs: During the year, the ACAFC envisaged a strategic 
digital networking portal aimed at fostering collaboration, connectivity and growth opportunities 
amongst Chartered Accountants and CA firms across India. The Request for Proposal (RFP) for 
the development of the portal was floated and the process for finalisation of the vendor was 
undertaken. Further, the homepage of the portal was soft-launched during the 448th meeting of 
the Council held on 6th—7th February 2026. 


e Outreach, Capacity Building & Stakeholder Sensitisation: During the year, the Committee 
undertook structured outreach initiatives and strategic consultative interactions on a pan-India 
basis to educate and sensitise stakeholders on emerging opportunities relating to growth, 
networking, collaboration and aggregation of CA firms. Through workshops, webinars, 


[भाग त-खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 


239 


mentorship sessions and one-on-one consultative interactions on “Big Indian Firms”, practical 
insights and guidance were provided to strengthen the capabilities of CA firms, address practical 
challenges and align the profession with evolving global best practices. 


e Webinars/Seminars/Workshops/ Programmes 


The Committee organized a series of 7 programmes across the country to promote the 
aggregation and growth of Chartered Accountant firms. These included webinars, seminars, 
workshops, outreach programmes, consultative interactions, a residential refresher course, and 
a half-day conference on themes such as aggregation of CA firms, networking and capacity 
building, scaling up professional firms, opportunities for CAs as independent directors, and the 
“Big Indian Firm: The Aggregation Blueprint” initiative. The programmes were conducted in both 
virtual and physical modes at various locations including Mumbai, Jaipur, Surat, Vadodara, 
Prayagraj, Rishikesh, Guwahati, Hisar, New Delhi, Faridabad, and other cities, facilitating 
stakeholder engagement and fostering discussions on strengthening and expanding the 
professional practice landscape in India. 


7.4 MEMBERS IN PRACTICE COMMITTEE (MPC) 


The Members in Practice Committee (MPC) (Earlier named as Committee for Members in Practice), a 
non-standing Committee of ICAI, was established with the primary objective of revitalising the practice 
portfolios of practitioners and firms through a suite of targeted initiatives. Aligned with ICAI’s 
overarching mission to cultivate globally competent professionals, the CMP undertakes multifaceted 
initiatives aimed at the capacity building of Chartered Accountants in practice, and regularly organises 
refresher courses, workshops, conferences, seminars, certificate courses, brainstorming sessions and 
interactive forums to strengthen the practice portfolio of members and enhance their visibility. 


(I) Capacity Building Measures in Identified Thrust Areas 


As part of the approved Action Plan for 2025-26, the Committee identified five emerging thrust areas 
for developing new avenues of professional growth for practising Chartered Accountants - GST 
Litigation, Real Estate Consultancy, Customs & Foreign Trade Policy (FTP), MSME Advisory and 
Accounting Outsourcing — and conceptualised structured workshop formats under each area. These 
formats were also popularised amongst the Branches of ICAI to encourage them to conduct the 
workshops for the benefit of members in practice. The workshops conducted under these formats 
include: 


e 6-Day Workshop on GST Dispute Mechanism (Strategies & Advocacy): Conducted jointly 
with the GST & Indirect Taxes Committee, this non-residential workshop is a focused training 
programme for Chartered Accountants engaged in advising taxpayers, drafting responses to 
departmental notices and representing clients in adjudication and appellate proceedings, offering 
hands-on learning through technical sessions, case studies, drafting exercises and mock tribunal 
hearings. Six such workshops were successfully organised. 

e Two-Day Workshop on “Communication Business Skills for Global Outsourcing”: A 
forward-looking programme equipping Chartered Accountants with the communication and 
interpersonal skills required to excel in global outsourcing, shared services and virtual work 
settings, integrating practical learning, case studies, real-life simulations and Al-driven 
applications. A physical batch was conducted at the Ahmedabad Branch of WIRC on ist—2nd 
November 2025. 

e Webinar Series — “Bridging the Mindset Gap”: A thought-driven webinar initiative going 
beyond technical mastery to strengthen the inner foundation of professional success - attitude, 
ethics and values — nurturing a growth-oriented mindset, ethical leadership and value-driven 
decision-making. The Committee successfully organised 27 such webinars (Mondays, 4:00-6:00 
p.m.). 

e Six-Day Workshop on Real Estate Advisory and Practice Setup: Developed jointly with 
the Committee on Commercial Laws, Economic Advisory & NPO Cooperative, the workshop covers 
the pre-development, development and post-development phases of consultancy opportunities, 


240 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SEc.4] 


offering an integrated understanding of RERA compliance, taxation (GST and Income-tax), due 
diligence, project financing, accounting and audit of real estate entities, Joint Development 
Agreements, legal documentation, valuation methodologies and revenue recognition under Ind 
AS i5. Two such workshops were successfully organised. 

e Two-Day Workshop on “Practice Opportunities in Customs & Foreign Trade Policy 
(FTP)”: Provides a comprehensive and practical understanding of the customs and international 
trade framework governing India’s import-export ecosystem, including legal provisions, 
procedures, valuation mechanisms and benefit structures under the Customs Act and the FTP, 
enabling members to translate conceptual knowledge into strategic advisory capability. 

e Four-Day Workshop on “Practice Opportunities in MSME”: A comprehensive learning 
initiative on the legal, financial and compliance ecosystem governing MSMEs, building 
professional capacity in advisory, consultancy and financing functions while familiarising members 
with Government schemes, incentives and policy frameworks for MSME growth. 

e Two-Day Workshop on “CA’s Toolkit: Practical Guide to Appeals & Representation”: 
Equips members with practical skills in drafting, representation and management of tax and legal 
proceedings across Direct and Indirect Tax laws and allied legislations — including search/seizure 
procedures, compliance under the Black Money, Benami and PMLA Acts, and representation 
before appellate forums and tribunals. A workshop was organised at the Hyderabad Branch of 
SIRC on 20th—2ist December 2025. 


(II) Other Capacity Building Initiatives 


e Practice ki Paathshala — Flagship Initiative: The flagship programme, designed to enhance 
professional competencies in practice management, technology integration, and networking and 
growth, was revamped from the earlier 3-day residential format to a more flexible 4-day 
residential/non-residential refresher format exclusively for practising Chartered Accountants, 
blending practice management, technology adoption (including AI and automation training), 
personal development and leadership skills. Nine batches of the programme were successfully 
organised. 

e “Grow Your Practice” Webinar Series: A series equipping Chartered Accountants with the 
latest tools, strategies and insights to expand their practices, covering business development, 
digital transformation, client management and emerging opportunities; seven webinars were 
successfully conducted. 

e Career Readiness Programme for New CAs: Organised jointly with the Committee for 
Members in Industry & Business, this 6-day virtual programme (i8 hours) empowers newly 
qualified Chartered Accountants with informed career decision-making, with a focused deep-dive 
into industry and independent practice. An online batch was organised from 24th to 27th 
December 2025. 

e Pathway to Practice — 7-Day Residential Training Programme for COP Applicants: The 
Committee revamped the structure of this comprehensive residential programme, tailored for 
first-time Certificate of Practice applicants, covering practice management, regulatory 
compliance, client engagement, technology adoption and ethical standards, conducted at the 
ICAI Centres of Excellence at Jaipur and Hyderabad. 

e One-Day Workshop on “Reimagining the CA Profession: Emerging Practice 
Opportunities in a Globalised Economy”: Conceptualised to sensitise members, particularly 
SMPs, to emerging high-growth domains — including GCCs, GIFT City, international taxation and 
cross-border advisory — and to facilitate a mindset shift from compliance-oriented practice to 
value-driven advisory services. Two such workshops were organised. 

e Two-Day Workshop on “Grow Your Practice 2026”: A thought-leadership initiative 
empowering SMPs to evolve into future-ready, high-value advisory and entrepreneurial practices, 
anchored in innovation, ethics, digital transformation and Al-enabled workflows. Three such 
workshops were organised. 


(III) Certificate Courses 


The Committee revamped its two Certificate Courses:

7.5 MSME & START-UP COMMITTEE 


Micro, Small and Medium Enterprises and Start-ups are the backbone of the Indian economy and key 
drivers of employment, innovation, and inclusive growth on the path to Viksit Bharat. The Institute of 
Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) has consistently aligned its initiatives with the nation's priorities 
and constituted a dedicated MSME & Startup committee to act as a dedicated enabler for small 
businesses and new-age enterprises. MSME & Startup Committee is focused on empowering MSMEs 
and start-ups and remains committed to strengthening India's entrepreneurial ecosystem by supporting 
MSMEs and startups through knowledge dissemination, capacity building, policy advocacy, and 
professional guidance. In the year 2025-26, the Committee has undertaken a wide range of initiatives 
e.g. publications, seminars, conferences, webinars, outreach programmes, and_ stakeholder 
engagement to promote excellence, foster innovation, and contribute meaningfully to the sustainable 
growth and competitiveness of India's MSME and startup ecosystem. 


242 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SEc.4] 


The key activities and achievements of the Committee during the period under report are as under: 
(I) Flagship Events 


e ICAI MSME Mahotsav 2025: Conducted on 27th June 2025 across 40+ ICAI Branches 
nationwide, supporting nearly 20,000 MSMEs through 400+ advisory helpdesks, with the 
participation of Ministers and senior officials. The initiative earned recognition from the World, 
Asia and India Books of Records for the “Maximum MSME Mentorship Felicitations in a Day”. 

e ICAI MSME & Startup Conclave 2025: Held on 26th—27th June 2025 at Mumbai to mark 
International MSME Day, with 3,000+ delegates, 9+ sessions, 60+ speakers and 38+ exhibitors. 
65 start-up pitches were made, with 3 start-ups securing funding. The event was supported by 
the Ministry of MSME, NSIC and CAIT, and the ICAI MSME Helpdesk offered on-the-spot 
mentoring. 

e ICAI MSME Connect — Stakeholders Strategic Meet 2025: Held on 24th October 2025 at 
New Delhi with 80+ participants and representation from 60+ ministries, regulators, financial 
institutions and incubators, focusing on MSME and start-up challenges, digital transformation and 
innovation, and exploring strategic collaborations and MoUs to strengthen the MSME ecosystem. 


(II) Initiatives towards Partner in Nation Building 


e Collaboration with the Ministry of MSME: The Committee collaborated with the Ministry of 
MSME to strengthen MSME capabilities nationwide, including support in conducting 
Entrepreneurship & Skill Development Programmes (EAP, E-SDP, MDP) with Development 
Facilitation Offices, leveraging ICAI’s network of 85 Branches, 5 Regional Councils and 50+ 
overseas Chapters, and conducting national seminars, financial management sessions and 
awareness campaigns at Kolkata, Mandla, Jabalpur and Solan. 


e SPARK — The i00K Collective Program: A formal association with the Women’s Collective 
Forum was entered into through a Letter of Engagement, with ICAI as knowledge partner of the 
SPARK — The I00K Collective Program led by Smt. Smriti Irani Ji. The pilot programme was held 
on 24th September 2025 at New Delhi, including an ICAI expert session on Income-tax and GST, 
with the participation of over 350 women entrepreneurs, reinforcing support to women-led 
MSMEs and start-ups. 


e Vision Rajasthan 2026: The Committee participated in the “Vision Rajasthan 2026” mega 
exhibition held from 9th to 2ist January 2026 at Sirohi, Rajasthan (an Aspirational District 
identified by NITI Aayog), setting up an exhibition stall to create awareness on corporate 
governance, MCA2i, Ease of Doing Business, MSME formalisation, entrepreneurship and 
compliance, supporting the outreach initiatives of the Ministry of Corporate Affairs. 


e Networking Meet: A Networking Meet on “Digitalising the Procure-to-Pay Process for MSMEs: 
Role of Chartered Accountants” was organised on 7th January 2026 at ICAI Tower, BKC, 
Mumbai, featuring industry experts from NPCI Bharat BillPay Limited and Tally, providing practical 
insights into the digitalisation of the procure-to-pay ecosystem and highlighting the critical role 
of Chartered Accountants in advising MSMEs on digital adoption, regulatory compliance and 
internal controls. 


(IIIT) Memoranda of Understanding

7.6 COMMITTEE ON PROMOTING WORK LIFE BALANCE 


The Committee on Promoting Work-Life Balance (now merged in Beneficiary Schemes Directorate), in 
the year 2025-26, undertook a series of focused and impactful initiatives aimed at integrating wellness, 
resilience, and sustainable professional practices within the Chartered Accountants fraternity. Guided 
by the belief that a healthy professional is a productive professional, the Committee worked towards 
embedding wellbeing as a routine and non-negotiable part of professional life, rather than an occasional 
afterthought. 


Various Initiatives undertaken by the Committee 
(I) Year-Round Wellness Initiatives and Activities 


Through the observance of global wellness days such as World Laughter Day, World Bicycle Day, the 
lith International Yoga Day 2025, and World Meditation Day 2025, the Committee underscored that 
physical, mental, and emotional wellness are essential pillars of long-term professional 
excellence. These observances were designed to spark reflection among members and students on the 
importance of pausing, recharging, and reconnecting with themselves amid demanding professional 
schedules. Notably, the Committee's efforts were recognized and ICAI’s name was recorded in Asia 
Book of Records, 2025, for organizing yoga sessions across the maximum number of branches on the 
occasion of International Yoga Day ,2025. This recognition stands as a testament to extensive 
participation of members and students and the Committee's success in mobilizing the CA fraternity 
nationwide. 


(II) Fit CA Movement 


In alignment with the Hon'ble Prime Minister's Fit India Movement, the Committee launched the 'Fit CA 
Movement initiative, offering daily virtual Yoga and Zumba sessions, accessible at the click of a 
button. Simple in design yet impactful in its reach, this initiative aimed to encourage Chartered 
Accountants to integrate fitness into their daily routines despite demanding professional commitments. 
By promoting a culture of health and wellbeing, the initiative contributed to the broader vision of a 
Viksit Bharat. 


(IIT) Weekly Webinar Series 


By adopting a digital-first approach, the Committee organized impactful virtual webinars on a weekly 
basis, led by domain experts, enabling nationwide participation and accessibility regardless of 
geographical location. These webinars covered diverse dimensions of work-life balance, with the 
objective of encouraging Chartered Accountants to adopt sustainable lifestyle habits, manage stress 
effectively, set healthy professional boundaries, and cultivate resilience while continuing to excel in 
their careers. 


(IV) Memorandum of Understanding with The Art of Living 


The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI), through its Committee on Promoting Work-Life 
Balance, entered into a Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) with The Art of Living on 0 February, 


[भाग ता---खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 245 


2026. This partnership aims to promote the holistic development of members and students by 
enhancing their life skills, emotional resilience, ethical values, and overall wellbeing, thereby 
contributing to the continued advancement of the profession. The MoU underscores ICAI's commitment 
to nurturing professionals who are not only technically proficient but also guided by integrity, 
accountability, and a strong sense of social responsibility. Through this partnership, the ICAI seeks to 
encourage initiatives focused on overall well-being, stress management, and inner development, while 
further strengthening the ethical foundation essential for the accounting profession and nation- 
building. 


Through these collective efforts, the Committee encouraged the CA fraternity to prioritise wellness, 
health, and personal passions alongside professional excellence, positioning work-life balance as a 
cornerstone of resilient and future-ready professionals. 


7.7 GLOBAL TRADE & SERVICES COMMITTEE 


The Global Trade & Services Committee (erstwhile Development of International Trade, Services & 
WTO Directorate) of ICAI is committed to strengthening India’s presence in global services trade by 
promoting international collaboration, enhancing professional opportunities and positioning accounting 
and finance as key export services in the evolving global economy. The Committee works closely with 
government bodies, industry stakeholders and international institutions to facilitate trade integration, 
policy engagement and global professional mobility. The Committee's action plan for the Council Year 
2026-27 focuses on five key areas: Trade & Market Integration; Global Services & Professional Mobility; 
Policy & Government Affairs; GCC & Emerging Global Ecosystems; and Capacity Building. 


(I) Initiatives towards Partner in Nation Building 


e Extension of Most Favoured Nation (MFN) treatment to New Zealand: The Council, at 
its 447th Meeting held on i0th—lith December 2025, considered the request received from the 
Ministry of Commerce and Industry regarding the extension of MFN treatment to New Zealand 
under the ongoing India-New Zealand Free Trade Agreement (FTA) negotiations, insofar as 
professional accounting services are concerned. 

e ITEC Training Programmes under the aegis of the Ministry of External Affairs: The 2nd 
ITEC Training Programme, on the theme “Frontiers of Global Accounting: Mastering Digital 
Transformation”, was conducted from i5th to 26th September 2025 with 29 delegates from 7 
countries, including Bhutan, Botswana, Fiji, Ghana, the Kingdom of Eswatini, Laos, Liberia, Mali, 
Mauritius, Nepal, Rwanda, Sierra Leone, South Africa, Sri Lanka, South Sudan, Tanzania and 
Uzbekistan. Further strengthening global engagement, the 3rd ITEC Training Programme, 
themed “Engaging Public Accountants in the 2ist Century”, was held from 42th to 23rd January 
2026 and attended by i8 participants from ii countries. These initiatives reflect ICAI’s 
commitment to fostering knowledge exchange, strengthening capacity building and promoting 
international cooperation in accounting and auditing. 

e Chintan Shivir 2025 on Professional Services: ICAI, in collaboration with the Ministry of 
Commerce and Industry and the Services Export Promotion Council (SEPC), organised a Chintan 
Shivir on Professional Services titled “Expanding Global Horizons: Opportunities for Indian 
Professionals” on 23rd December 2025, witnessing the participation of over 00 delegates from 
industry, the professional fraternity and Ministries. Four focused sessions covered making 
professionals globally ready; strengthening international mobility through MRAs and MoUs; 
developing global networks through professional chapters abroad; and leveraging FTAs to boost 
exports of professional services. 


(II) Participation at International Platforms 


e Participation at Accountex London: ICAI showcased its activities and initiatives in the field 
of accounting and finance at Accountex, held on i4th—i5th May 2025 in London, through a 
dedicated 8 sq.m. stall. The stall was visited by Mr. Malcolm Bacchus, President, ICAEW, and 
Ms. Ami Copeland, CEO, Institute of Certified Bookkeepers, reflecting strong international interest 
in ICAI’s initiatives.

7.8 PROFESSIONAL DEVELOPMENT COMMITTEE (PDC) 


The Professional Development Committee (PDC), established in 962 with a mission to enhance the 
skill sets of members of the Institute in existing and new areas, actively engages with the Government 
and regulatory authorities to promote the professional opportunities of Chartered Accountants across 
diverse sectors. 


The key activities of the Committee during the period under report are as under: 
(I) Meetings with Regulators and Other Bodies 


e Reserve Bank of India: Four meetings were held with the Executive Director, Department of 
Supervision, RBI — on ist April 2025 (Mumbai: appointment of SCAs/SBAs, rotation policy, audit 
coverage); 8th April 2025 (Delhi: Governance Framework of Audit in Public Sector Banks); and 
i5th April 2026 (Mumbai: strengthening audit framework in PSBs, technology in assurance, ICAI 
certifications for banking assignments). A meeting was also held with the CGM, Department of 
Supervision, RBI on 29th May 2025 (structured training for Audit Committee Chairmen, regular 
ICAI-RBI meetings, NBFC compliances). More than 45 State Level Coordination Committee 
(SLCC) meetings convened by RBI across India were attended by ICAI representatives during the 
year. 

e NABARD: Three meetings were held with the Chief General Manager, Institutional Development 
Department, NABARD - on 3ist July 2025 (audit of Cooperative Banks and RRBs); 27th February 
2026 (audit of StCBs, CtCBs and RRBs, interactive meets for auditors); and a follow-up online 
meeting on 7th April 2026 (allotment issues for SAs post-merger of RRBs). 

e Ministry of Cooperation / CRCS: Three meetings were held with the CRCS and Ministry of 
Cooperation — on 30th April 2025 (challenges in Concurrent Audit assignments); I3th May 2025 
(formulation of auditing/accounting norms for Multi-State Co-operative Societies, jointly with 
C&AG, RBI and NCUI); and i8th March 2026 (empanelment of firms for MSCS concurrent audit). 

¢ Office of C&AG: A meeting was held on 0th November 2025 with the Deputy CAG (Commercial 
& RC) regarding feedback on the existing empanelment policy and suggestions for new norms 
following the notification of the Aggregation of LLPs Guidelines, 2024. Majority of ICAI’s 
submissions were accepted and incorporated in the 2026-27 policy. 

e SEBI: A meeting was held with the Executive Director, SEBI at SEBI Bhawan, Mumbai on 26th 
November 2025 to address issues arising from the Notice Inviting Applications for Empanelment 
of Forensic Auditors issued on 4th November 2025. 

e IRDAI: A meeting was held with Member (Finance & Investment), IRDAI on i2th March 2026 
at Hyderabad to deliberate on empanelment of audit firms, cybersecurity and compliance audits, 
standardisation of regulatory certifications by Chartered Accountants and organisation of an 
Insurance Summit for statutory auditors.

7.9 PROFESSIONAL SKILL ENRICHMENT COMMITTEE (PSEC) 


The Professional Skills Enrichment Committee (now merged in members in practice committee) focused 
on empowering Chartered Accountants with contemporary professional, behavioural, managerial and 
leadership skills required to excel in a dynamic and evolving professional environment. Through a wide 
range of initiatives encompassing skill enhancement, structured learning, self-assessment, digital 
learning, wellness and professional development, the Committee promoted continuous learning and 
holistic professional growth, enabling members to remain future-ready and aligned with emerging 
business and professional requirements.

8. OTHER COMMITTEES/ BOARDS 
8.4 AUDIT COMMITTEE 


The Audit Committee of the Institute was established in April 200i by the Council as a non-standing 
committee with a clearly defined mandate. Since 2009, it has been chaired exclusively by a Government 
Nominee to ensure greater independence. To further enhance its autonomy, members of the Executive 
Committee and the Finance Committee are not permitted to serve on the Central Audit Committee. 
Additionally, five Regional Audit Committees — Southern, Western, Central, Northern and Eastern — 
support the Committee’s work, ensuring regional representation and effective oversight. 


The Committee is responsible for overseeing the financial reporting process and ensuring the accuracy, 
integrity and transparency of the Institute’s financial disclosures. It reviews audit reports, discusses 
observations with the auditors, recommends corrective actions and strives to strengthen the internal 
control environment. The Committee remains committed to good governance and continuous 
improvement in audit practices. 


Statutory Audit 


e Appointment of Statutory Auditors: In accordance with Section i8(5) of the Chartered 
Accountants Act, i949, the statutory auditors for ICAI’s Branches, Regional Councils, 
Decentralised Offices, Centre of Excellence and Head Office are appointed from a panel of two 
firms recommended by the Comptroller and Auditor General (C&AG) of India, effective from FY 
2022-23. This system ensures continuity and avoids audit delays. A status report on these 
appointments is regularly shared with the C&AG. 

e Audit Reporting Framework: The statutory auditors’ reporting framework is governed by 
Regulations 96 and 97 of the Chartered Accountants Regulations, 988. 

¢ Joint Statutory Auditors: The accounts of the ICAI Head Office are audited by two joint 
statutory auditors, bringing diverse expertise and ensuring comprehensive review and 
accountability. 


Internal Audit 


e Internal Audit Coverage: A robust internal audit mechanism is in place for all major units, 
including branches with over a thousand members, Regional Councils, Decentralised Offices, the 
Centre of Excellence and the Head Office. The internal audit is conducted under the supervision 
of the Audit Committee, which ensures transparency and auditor independence. 


e Appointment through 50: Internal auditors are appointed through a transparent process 
involving an open invitation for Expressions of Interest (50) from CA firms. Selection is based 
on an objective evaluation and ranking system. Audits are conducted biannually and cover both 
financial and compliance aspects, as per the prescribed scope and reporting format. Audit reports 
are reviewed by the respective Regional or Central Audit Committees for follow-up action. 


256 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SEc.4] 


Annual Accounts 


e Financial Statement Format: ICAI prepares its financial statements in accordance with the 
illustrative format for Not-for-Profit Organisations, as per the Technical Guide issued by the 
Institute. The format is customised to enhance relevance and clarity for stakeholders. 


e Approval Process: The approval of audited financial statements follows a structured and 
statutory process under Section 8(5A) of the Chartered Accountants Act, 949, and Regulation 
200 of the Chartered Accountants Regulations, 988, as depicted below: 


8.2 MEMBERS IN PUBLIC SERVICE COMMITTEE 


The Members in Public Service Committee (MPSC) (earlier named as Committee for Members in 
Entrepreneurship and Public Services) serves as a dedicated platform for ICAI members engaged in 
public service or entrepreneurship. With a focus on promoting public service and entrepreneurship as 
career options for CAs, the Committee undertakes various initiatives to inspire and empower members. 
Many Chartered Accountants currently serve in senior roles across government departments, ministries, 
legislatures, the judiciary, regulatory bodies and entrepreneurial ventures, actively contributing to 
governance, economic growth and the nation’s development. The Committee organises 
Residential/Interactive Meets of ICAI members in public service to solicit their suggestions on matters 
of national importance and continuously explores the opportunities that lie in entrepreneurship for CA 
members through mentorship sessions, orientation and training programmes, webinars, seminars and 
regional meets. 


The key activities during the period are as under: 
(I) Interactive and Mentorship Programmes

interactive meet held on 9th June 2025 at New Delhi, attended by 25 members and featuring 
distinguished speakers including CA. Navneet Kumar Sehgal, IAS (Retd.), Chairman, Prasar 
Bharati; CA. K. Jegadesan, IPS, Special Commissioner of Police (Traffic); and Shri Ankur Aggarwal 
on careers in the Civil Services, along with CA. (Dr.) Nitin Soni on entrepreneurship. The event 
was graced by the President, ICAI, the Vice-President, ICAI, the Secretary, ICAI and CA. Hans 
Raj Chugh, CCM. 


e “Chartered Minds in Public Service: Inspiring the Next Generation” — Jaipur: An

8.3 FINANCIAL MARKETS AND INVESTORS’ AWARENESS COMMITTEE (FM&IAC) 


The Committee on Financial Markets and Investors’ Awareness provides suggestions on Bills, 
Notifications, Circulars, Consultation Papers, and other documents relating to financial markets to the 
Government and Regulators. The Committee regularly interacts with the Ministry of Corporate Affairs, 
the Reserve Bank of India, the Securities and Exchange Board of India, the Insurance Regulatory and 
Development Authority, the Department of Non-Banking Finance Companies of RBI, and Stock 
Exchanges on matters relating to financial markets, the role of Chartered Accountants, and investor 
protection. 


Partner in Nation Building 


To create awareness among the public and ensure investor protection, the Committee conducted 00 
Investor Awareness Programmes through Programme Organising Units of ICAI, including Branches, 
Regional Councils, and Study Circles, to enhance investors’ understanding of developments in financial 
markets under the aegis of the Investor Education and Protection Fund Authority (IEPFA), Ministry of 
Corporate Affairs (MCA).

8.5 EDITORIAL BOARD 


The Editorial Board is a specialised non-standing committee of ICAI entrusted with enriching the 
professional knowledge ecosystem of the Institute through its flagship publication, The Chartered 
Accountant. Widely regarded as one of the most respected professional journals in the country, the 
publication reaches more than 400,000 readers through its print and digital editions. As the voice of 
ICAI's thought leadership, the journal serves as a dynamic platform for disseminating contemporary 
developments, expert insights, research-driven perspectives, and emerging trends shaping the 
accounting and financial profession, reflecting the Institute’s commitment to professional excellence, 
continuous learning, and intellectual advancement. 


260 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SEc.4] 


Under the guidance of the Editorial Board, the publication strengthens ICAI’s engagement with 
members, students, regulators, and stakeholders, while reinforcing the Institute’s brand and leadership 
in the global professional arena. With relevant, insightful, and future-oriented content, the Board plays 
a pivotal role in connecting the profession with evolving practices in finance, governance, sustainability, 
technology, and regulatory frameworks. 


(I) Enabling India’s Growth Through Professional Knowledge 


Through the monthly journal The Chartered Accountant, the Editorial Board has consistently contributed 
to India’s development journey by fostering a culture of knowledge, innovation, and professional 
excellence within the accounting fraternity. By bringing forward contemporary issues, emerging global 
trends, regulatory developments, and transformative ideas, the Board equips ICAI members to navigate 
an evolving economic landscape with competence and vision. The journal serves as a powerful medium 
for promoting informed decision-making, ethical practices, and forward-looking professional thought, 
strengthening the role of Chartered Accountants as key contributors to economic progress, governance, 
and nation building. 


(II) Advancing Professional Excellence 


During the period, The Chartered Accountant journal showcased a rich spectrum of articles addressing 
diverse and contemporary issues relevant to the accounting profession. Alongside its regular 
professional features, the journal brought out special editions focused on significant and emerging 
areas such as MSMEs, Leadership, GST, Technology, Sustainability, Startups, and Capital Markets, 
among other key technical subjects. These thematic issues were curated to enhance members’ 
professional knowledge, broaden perspectives, and equip them to remain future-ready in a rapidly 
evolving environment. 


(III) Member and Student Initiatives — Inclusions in the Journal 


During the period, 207 articles on various themes and professional topics were included in the ICAI 
Journal, in addition to regular features covering updates on the Institute's activities in coordination with 
various ICAI Committees. The July 2025 edition featured a diverse range of special write-ups and 
knowledge-driven articles focused on ethics, sustainability, artificial intelligence, digital transformation, 
leadership, wellness, financial innovation, women empowerment, and nation building, with 
contributions from eminent professionals, policymakers, and global leaders. The issue also included 
sections such as Practice to Policy, Global Footprint, In Conversation, and Lifestyle and Wellness, and 
carried inspirational CA Day messages from 39 eminent dignitaries, including the Prime Minister of 
India, Governors, Union Ministers, and Chief Ministers. 


(IV) Digital Versions of The Chartered Accountant — Upgraded for Convenience 


e Journal in PDF/Flipbook Format: The journal continues to be hosted in PDF/Flipbook format, 
with digital archives available on the ICAI website from July 2002 onwards. 

e Journal on Digital Learning Hub: The electronic version of the journal is available as a user- 
friendly e-magazine on the ICAI website and the Digital Learning Hub, supporting the ICAI 
Learning Management System and the Institute’s sustainability drive. 

¢ Journal Highlight Emailers: Highlights of every issue, along with the President’s Message, are 
mass-emailed to all members in capsule form. 

¢ Journal on Mobile: The e-journal is accessible on iOS and Android devices through the ICAI 
website and the ICAI Mobile App. 


8.6 INFRASTRUCTURE DEVELOPMENT COMMITTEE (IDC) 
The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) has continuously expanded and strengthened 


its infrastructure since the inauguration of its Headquarters at Indraprastha Marg, New Delhi, in 954. 
To ensure systematic planning, monitoring and standardisation of infrastructure development across 


[भाग ता---खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 26l 


its Headquarters, Regional Councils, Branches, Centres of Excellence, Decentralised Offices and other 
establishments, the Infrastructure Development Committee (IDC) was constituted in 20i4 as a Special 
Purpose Non-Standing Committee under the authority of the Council. The Council also approved the 
Infrastructure Policy for Branches and Regional Councils in 20i4, providing a comprehensive framework 
for infrastructure planning and development. Since then, the IDC has been responsible for assessing 
infrastructure requirements, formulating and reviewing policies, examining proposals for land 
acquisition, construction, renovation and redevelopment, monitoring project implementation, and 
advising the Council on all infrastructure-related matters, thereby ensuring planned, transparent and 
sustainable infrastructure development across the Institute.

8.8 MANAGEMENT COMMITTEE 


Management Committee, constituted in 20i5 as non-standing Committee of the Council, is mandated 
to consider matters pertaining to formation of Branches, setting up of Chapters abroad, MoUs/ MRAs 
with national/ international bodies, appointment of central auditors of ICAI, matters referred by the 
Central Government and other regulatory bodies, proposals for amendments in the Chartered 
Accountants Act, 949, Rules and Regulations framed thereunder, Regional Councils and Branches 
matters, Members/CA firms/LLPs/mergers/demergers/networking related matters and proposals 
received from other committees/departments of the Institute having administrative and policy 
implications and making its recommendations to the Council wherever required. 


8.9 PUBLIC RELATIONS COMMITTEE (PRC) 


The Public Relations Committee has been working to develop, strengthen and enhance the image of 
the ICAI as a premier accountancy body and the sole regulatory authority for the profession of 
Chartered Accountancy in India & globally through various ways and means, as considered appropriate 
within framework of the CA Act. The Committee also endeavours to foster strong relationships with the 
media and other stakeholders, in coordination with various Committees of ICAI, to facilitate effective 
communication, disseminate information and enhance the visibility of the Institute. Recognising the 
media as a strategic platform for communicating with the public, policymakers and key stakeholders, 
ICAI has proactively leveraged it to effectively convey its views and decisions. The Public Relations 
Committee has not only expanded ICAI’s media footprint in terms of reach and volume but has also 
built enduring relationships with key national media. 


The Public Relations Committee undertook extensive communication, media engagement, branding and 
outreach initiatives during the period to enhance the visibility of ICAI’s initiatives and strengthen public 
awareness of the accounting profession.

Overseas Chapters actively participated. These initiatives together earned ICAI recognition from the 
Asia Book of Records for the maximum number of branches of a single organisation conducting Yoga 
simultaneously.

8.44 STRATEGY, PERSPECTIVE PLANNING AND MONITORING COMMITTEE (SPP&MC) 


The Strategy Perspective Planning and Monitoring Committee is a non-standing committee of the 
Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) serves a critical role in shaping long-term ICAI Vision 
and objectives. The primary purpose of this committee is to engage in strategic planning, ensuring that 
the ICAI develops and implements effective strategies to meet its goal. The committee is committed to 
effective risk management, identifying potential challenges, and adapting strategies to navigate a 
dynamic business environment. By analysing various perspective, trends and factors, the committee 
aim to provide a comprehensive outlook for the organization’s future. Additionally, it plays a critical role 
in monitoring the execution of strategic initiatives, assessing their impact, and making adjustments as 
necessary. 


Preparation of Research Report ICAI Vision 2049 

Collaboration with IIMA 

In pursuit of a forward-looking strategy for the Institute, the ICAI initiated the development of ICAI 
Vision 2049 through a collaborative engagement with the Indian Institute of Management Ahmedabad 
(IIMA). This collaboration aimed to outline a comprehensive roadmap that aligns the Institute’s long- 
term goals with emerging global, technological, and regulatory trends and Research Report has been 
submitted by IIM Ahmedabad. 

8.2 ICAI CORPORATE MITRA COMMITTEE 


The ICAI Corporate Mitra Committee has been constituted for the Corporate Mitra Project of the 
Government of India to undertake coordination, outreach, awareness creation and other activities for 


[भाग त-खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 


207 


its effective implementation, ensure access to training through the Regions, Branches, Chapters and 
offices of ICAI, including periodic continuing training, capacity building and skill enrichment for 
Corporate Mitra, enlist human, logistic and other resources required for the initiative, and constitute 
working groups or invite experts, academicians and industry leaders for its implementation, while 
contributing to the formulation of the overall scheme for the Project. The ICAI Corporate Mitra 
Committee continued its efforts towards supporting Government initiatives aimed at enhancing 
employability, skill development and ease of doing business. During the year, the Committee actively 
engaged with the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA) in connection with the Prime Minister Internship 
Scheme and the proposed Corporate Mitra Scheme. 


(I) Prime Minister Internship Scheme (PMIS) 


The Prime Minister Internship Scheme (PMIS), launched by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs under the 
Union Budget 2024-25, aims to provide structured, paid and industry-linked internship opportunities to 
youth across India. Recognising the importance of experiential learning and practical training, ICAI 
extended its support to the initiative and was invited by MCA to participate in Pilot Phase III of the 
Scheme. 


Key features of the Scheme include: 


e Internship allowance of २9,000 per month, comprising %900 by the participating firm and %8,i00 by 
MCA. 

e One-time financial assistance of २6,000 by MCA. 

e Internship duration of 9 months. 

e Eligibility for graduates aged between 8 and 25 years, subject to prescribed criteria. 

e Implementation through a dedicated single-window portal administered by MCA. 


The Committee continued its efforts to support wider outreach and participation, particularly in Tier-II 
and Tier-III cities, thereby contributing towards the development of industry-ready professionals. 


(II) Corporate Mitra Scheme 


The Corporate Mitra Scheme, announced by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs in the Union Budget 2026- 
27, seeks to strengthen the MSME ecosystem by creating a pool of trained and certified 
paraprofessionals capable of assisting enterprises in regulatory, financial and governance matters. 


Key features of the Scheme include: 


e The Scheme envisages six months of structured online academic learning followed by six 
months of on-the-job training. 

e ICAI developed the module on Accounting, Taxation, Banking and Financial Management and 
submitted the same to MCA for consideration. 

e The Scheme is proposed to commence with 2,000 participants, including 200 participants from 
the North-Eastern Region. 

e Successful candidates will be certified as “Corporate Mitra” upon completion of assessments 
and on-the-job training and will be equipped to function as MSME compliance facilitators. 

e Academic content is proposed to be made available in multiple Indian languages to promote 
wider accessibility and inclusiveness. 


The Committee contributed towards the development of the academic framework of the Scheme. The 
module on Accounting, Taxation, Banking and Financial Management was developed by the subject 
faculties of the Board of Studies (Academic), ICAI and submitted to MCA for consideration. The Scheme 
is expected to strengthen ease of doing business, support MSME compliance requirements and create 
structured employment opportunities for youth across the country.

8.3. CENTRAL COUNCIL LIBRARY 


The Central Council Library of the Institute caters to the information requirements of its stakeholders. 
Its aim is to provide a comprehensive and up-to-date collection of primary and secondary print and 
non-print material to present and prospective members, students, research scholars and officials of 
ICAI. The Library has assumed greater responsibilities in serving Committees and Departments by 
imparting knowledge and valuable information through books, e-books, journals, magazines, online 
databases, print newspapers as well as e-newspapers, and is responsible for updating and providing 
the journals and books required for the work of various Committees. 


The Central Council Library is fully computerised and operates through the Liberty library management 
software. Library material, including the database of books, journals and articles, can be searched 
subject-wise, author-wise, title-wise, topic-wise, keyword-wise and publisher-wise. These records are 
available through the online search OPAC (Liberty) under “Central Council Library” on the ICAI website 
(www. icai.org). 


Under the column “Accountants’ Browser”, an index of articles relevant to the accounting profession is 
published every month in the journal ‘The Chartered Accountant’. The “Accountants’ Browser” is an 
index of important professional articles, with archives of past articles. Reference services are also 
provided by the Library to researchers, scholars, faculties, students and members. 


A number of online databases have been acquired by the Library, the details of which are available on 
www.icai.org under “Central Council Library”. These online knowledge databases have been installed 
in the Central Council Library and can be accessed in-house to facilitate the search for required material 
by members, faculties and research scholars. Several e-journals have also been subscribed to by the 
Library. The details of the new resources added in the Central Council Library at the Head Office and 
the Noida Library during the year (ist April 2025 to 3lst March 2026) are as under:

8.4 RIGHT TO INFORMATION ACT, 2005 


The basic objective of the Right to Information Act, 2005 is to empower citizens and promote 
transparency and accountability in the working of every public authority. The Institute of Chartered 
Accountants of India (ICAI), a statutory body set up by the Chartered Accountants Act, 949, is a public 
authority as envisaged under Section 2(h) of the RTI Act, 2005. In compliance with the provisions of 
the RTI Act, 2005 and the directions of the Central Information Commission, officers of the Institute 
have been designated as the Central Public Information Officer (CPIO), Central Assistant Public 
Information Officer, and First Appellate Authority (FAA). 


A large number of RTI applications are received by the RTI Cell, ICAI from students, members, other 
stakeholders and citizens, and are responded to in a timely manner. In terms of Section 4()(b) of the 
RTI Act, 2005, all necessary disclosures have been made by hosting them on the Institute’s website 
(www. icai.org), which are updated from time to time. 


[भाग ता---खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 269 


During the period 2025-26, a total of 2,29,758 applications were received. The large majority comprise 
requests for certified copies of answer sheets for the CA Foundation, Intermediate and Final 
examinations. In addition, a significant number of RTI applications pertaining to Disciplinary functions, 
HRD, Examinations and Administration were also received. The category-wise break-up is as under: 


Period Foundation | Intermediate | Final Others 
June to December 20,292 73,77 58,85 574 ,53,398

2026

Further, i hearings of the Central Information Commission were attended, and a significant number 
of orders of the First Appellate Authority were responded to during the year.

webcasts, CoED has further strengthened ICAI’s position as a credible and trusted knowledge partner
