9.3 CONTINUING PROFESSIONAL EDUCATION DIRECTORATE 


The Continuing Professional Education Committee (CPE) (earlier named as Continuing Professional 
Education Committee) is a non-standing Committee of the Council of the ICAI entrusted with the task 
of setting strategic directions and overseeing CPE activities of POUs, members, etc. under the directions 
of the Council. During the year under review, the erstwhile Continuing Professional Education 
Committee (CPEC) was restructured and redesignated as the “CPE Directorate (CPED)” of ICAI for the 
Council Year 2026-27. The Directorate has been entrusted with strengthening the Continuing 
Professional Education framework of the Institute through structured learning, policy reforms, digital 
transformation and enhanced outreach initiatives aimed at ensuring the continuous professional 
development of members. 


The CPE Directorate continued to promote lifelong learning and professional excellence by organising 
and facilitating high-quality educational programmes across the country and abroad through its 
extensive network of Programme Organising Units (POUs).During the period under review, the 
Directorate held seven meetings, including meetings of the erstwhile CPEC and the newly constituted 
CPED, at New Delhi and Jaipur for the deliberation and implementation of key policy initiatives and 
reforms. 


(I) Expansion of the CPE Network 


One of the major achievements of the Directorate was the expansion of the CPE network in remote and 
underserved regions. A comprehensive exercise undertaken during the year identified 85 prospective 
locations, comprising 260 potential Study Chapters and 59 Study Groups at the district and tehsil 
levels. Consequent to this initiative, !7 new CPE POUs, including Study Circles, Study Chapters and 
Study Groups, were established, significantly enhancing the geographical reach and accessibility of 
professional education initiatives. 


(II) Reform-Oriented Initiatives 


The CPE Directorate undertook several reform-oriented initiatives approved by the Council to strengthen 
the CPE framework and improve operational efficiency. These included: (a) revision in the minimum 
fee structure for CPE programmes; (b) enhancement in the fixed asset purchase limits for Study 
Chapters; (c) rationalisation of seminar limits for Central Committees; (d) reduction in the minimum 


[भाग ता-खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 273 


duration of technical sessions for flagship events; (९) revision in the CPE hours requirements effective 
from Calendar Year 2026; and (f) simplification of exemption norms for members. Further, the 
Directorate facilitated voluntary contributions towards the Chartered Accountants’ Benevolent Fund 
(CABF) through the new DigiCA CPE Portal and reviewed the grant guidelines for Study Chapters to 
ensure improved accountability and the regular conduct of programmes. 


(III) DigiCA CPE Portal 


In order to modernise and streamline the CPE ecosystem, the Directorate progressed significantly 
towards the implementation of the DigiCA CPE Portal, a comprehensive digital platform aimed at 
ensuring automation, transparency, real-time attendance tracking, efficient programme approvals and 
a seamless experience for members and Programme Organising Units. The portal is expected to 
significantly improve programme management, monitoring, compliance and the accessibility of CPE 
activities. 


(IV) Knowledge Dissemination and Programmes

during the year.

9.4 DIGITAL RE-ENGINEERING AND TRANSFORMATION DIRECTORATE (DRTD) 


The Digital Re-Engineering and Transformation Directorate (DRTD) continued to drive ICAI's digital 
transformation agenda during the year, strengthening digital governance, automation and online 
service delivery for members, students and internal stakeholders. The key initiatives undertaken during 
the period under report are as under: 


(I) Enterprise Document Management System (DMS) Implementation across Regional 
Offices 


ICAI successfully completed a large-scale Document Management System (DMS) project for all five 
Regional Offices on a war footing, resulting in the digitisation and archival of over 8 crore documents. 
The initiative has significantly strengthened centralised record management, secure document retrieval, 
preservation of legacy records, operational efficiency and digital governance across the Institute. The 
project marks a major milestone towards ICAI’s vision of a paperless, technology-driven and efficiently 
managed institutional ecosystem. 


(II) Digital Credentials on DigiLocker 


ICAI enabled the issuance of Membership Identity Cards and Examination Marksheets through 
DigiLocker, strengthening secure digital access and paperless service delivery for members and 
students. The initiative facilitates anytime, anywhere availability of authenticated academic and 
professional credentials, aligned with the Government of India’s Digital India vision and an enhanced 
digital governance framework. 


(IIIT) Common Website Layout and Standardised Digital Identity for ICAI Branches through 
Centralised CMS 


ICAI continued its digital transformation initiative by implementing a centralised Content Management 
System (CMS) for Branch websites, enabling a common layout, a standardised design framework and 
a unified digital identity across the ICAI network. As on 30th April 2026, applications from 48 Branches 
were received, out of which 36 Branch websites were successfully developed and shared. The initiative 
ensures consistency in branding, improved accessibility, enhanced security, ease of content 
management and compliance with ICAI web policies. To support Branches in strengthening their digital 
presence, ICAI Headquarters bore the major cost towards website development, AWS cloud hosting 
and onboarding support, thereby enabling Branches to establish a professional and cost-effective digital 
ecosystem. 


[भाग ता---खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 275 


(IV) Launch of the ICAI Snapshots Portal for Centralised Digital Record Management 


ICAI launched the “Snapshots” Portal as a centralised digital repository and document management 
platform for Committees, Boards, Departments, Regional Councils, Branches and various ICAI 
portals/applications. The portal has been designed to facilitate the secure storage, systematic 
organisation, easy retrieval and efficient sharing of official documents, reports, circulars, meeting 
records, selected photographs and other institutional data. The platform also serves as a dedicated 
repository for MoU/MRA agreements executed with Governments, trade organisations, universities, 
professional bodies and technology partners, along with the list of ICAI portals and applications and 
Non-Disclosure Agreements (NDAs) with vendors. The initiative aims to strengthen digital governance, 
preserve institutional memory, improve operational efficiency and promote a paperless working 
environment across ICAI, while enhancing collaboration, accessibility, secure archival and the long- 
term preservation of important organisational records, strategic partnerships and key milestones of the 
Institute. 


(V) ENGAGE Platform — An Integrated Centralised Mass Communication Mechanism for 
ICAI’s Regional Councils and Branches 


The DRT Directorate developed ‘ENGAGE’, a centralised communication platform enabling ICAI Regional 
Councils and Branches to send mass emails and SMS to members and students within their jurisdiction 
in a secure and efficient manner. The platform has been rolled out to all Regional Councils and Branches 
after a round of demonstrations and training for the officers and staff of all five Regional Councils and 
Branches, in coordination with the RBA Directorate of ICAI. As on 30th April 2026, ENGAGE login 
credentials have been shared with all 86 Branches of ICAI, out of which 55 Branches are using the 
mass e-mail services and 26 Branches are using the SMS services through the platform. The DRTD 
Secretariat is organising periodic training sessions on the ENGAGE platform for all Regional Councils 
and Branches. 


(VI) ICAI Digital Learning Hub 


The ICAI Digital Learning Hub is an initiative aimed at leveraging technology to provide enhanced 
learning opportunities and resources to members, students and other stakeholders. By centralising 
educational materials and resources in a digital format, the Hub facilitates easy access to a wide range 
of learning content, including e-books, webinars, courses and interactive modules, promoting 
continuous professional development and ensuring that users can access high-quality educational 
content anytime and anywhere. The Hub has evolved over the years, with various customisations and 
alignment of ICAI processes enabled for the conduct of virtual and physical courses. It is currently 
being used by the Central Committees for Certificate Courses, Post-Qualification Courses, Refresher 
Courses, CPE events and online self-paced courses, and has been extended to ICAI Branches and 
Regional Councils for conducting their virtual CPE events and web lectures. The Digital Learning Hub 
broadly provides the following facilities to its stakeholders: 


e Knowledge repository for professional and academic learning, with content delivery providing 
access to courses, lectures and other learning materials. 

e Learning content in multiple formats (documents, PPTs, video, audio, SCORM) tailored to suit 
every learner. 

e Leader board feature showcasing credits achieved, and badges to members based on CPE hours 
earned. 

e Structured and Unstructured CPE hours credits for ICAI members. 

¢ Communities to share ideas. 

e Course access to Government officials, bank officials and MRA/MoU/SAFA members, along with 
Skill India courses for non-members. 

e Certificates generated for learners on successful completion of examinations.

9.5 ESTATE DEVELOPMENT DIRECTORATE (EDD) 


The Estate Development Directorate (EDD) administers matters relating to the release of Capital Grants 
to Units of ICAI, including Regional Councils, Branches, DCOs, and Centres of Excellence (COEs), for 
repair and renovation works, additions, internal modifications, and small construction activities up to 
200 lakhs. The several key initiatives towards infrastructure standardisation, sustainability, and digital 
governance, are given below:- 


(I) Infrastructure Standardisation 


Under the theme “My Estate, My Pride,” EDD launched Project SHIELD — the Sustainable Infrastructure 
Initiative — to promote sustainable and environmentally responsible infrastructure management across 
ICAI establishments nationwide. The project lays down a unified framework for branding, signage, and 
the upkeep of ICAI premises, ensuring that every Unit reflects a consistent and dignified institutional 
identity. As part of the brand building exercise, EDD continued the installation of uniform glow signage 
and standardised painting of ICAI buildings across the country, reinforcing visual consistency and 
strengthening the Institute’s public image. 


(II) Installation of Solar Power Plants 


In furtherance of the Institute’s commitment to clean energy and sustainability, EDD continued the 
installation of solar power plants at various ICAI establishments. The initiative aims to reduce the 


[भाग त-खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 


277 


Institute’s carbon footprint, lower energy costs, and demonstrate ICAI’s leadership in adopting 
renewable energy solutions across its nationwide network of offices. 


(III) Review of Safety and Accessibility Infrastructure 


EDD conducted a comprehensive review of firefighting systems, lift facilities, accessibility provisions for 
specially-abled persons, and rainwater harvesting systems across i29 ICAI Units. This exercise was 
undertaken to assess the current state of safety, accessibility, and environmental compliance 
infrastructure, and to identify Units requiring upgradation. The review forms the basis for a structured 
action plan to bring all Units up to uniform safety and accessibility standards. 


(IV) Automation of Directorate Processes 


EDD continued the automation of its processes through dedicated software for Capital Budget 
management, asset tracking, and workflow monitoring. The automation initiative has significantly 
enhanced efficiency, transparency, and governance in the processing and disbursement of Capital 
Grants, enabling real-time tracking of project status and financial utilisation across ICAI Units. 


(V) ICAI Reading Room Initiatives 


EDD undertook detailed surveys and collected status reports from ICAI Units to assess existing Reading 
Room infrastructure, student usage patterns, and operational feasibility. Based on the findings of this 
exercise, the ICAI Reading Room Policy 2025 was formulated and approved in August 2025. The policy 
introduces simplified norms, defines infrastructure standards, and streamlines approval procedures for 
the establishment and management of Reading Rooms across the country, ensuring a consistent and 
student-friendly experience at all locations. 


To support implementation of the policy, EDD launched Project SHABD — Students Hub for Aspirations 
and Building Dreams — for the creation of modern Reading Hubs equipped with improved study facilities 
and student-centric amenities. EDD also organised orientation sessions and webinars for Regional 
Councils, Branches, and student representatives on the provisions of the policy and the associated 
funding mechanisms, ensuring smooth and uniform adoption across the Institute’s network. 


9.6 FINANCIAL AND TAX LITERACY DIRECTORATE 


In line with the Government of India’s Vittiva Saksharta Abhiyaan, the Institute of Chartered 
Accountants of India (ICAI), through its Financial and Tax Literacy Directorate (now merged with 
Financial Markets and Investors’ Awareness Committee (FM&IAC), continued its efforts during 2025- 
26 to promote financial and tax literacy among the public. Guided by the motto ‘Har Jan, Har Man, 
Vittivagyan se Sampann’, the Directorate expanded its outreach through strategic collaborations with 
Government Departments, State Rural Livelihood Missions, educational institutions, and other 
organisations, while also leveraging digital and mass media platforms to enhance financial awareness 
across diverse sections of society, including students, teachers, government officials, women self-help 
groups, agriculturists, senior citizens, enforcement personnel, scientists, scholars, housewives, and local 
communities. 


(I) Key Initiatives 


e Financial Literacy for Government Functionaries 

The Directorate conducted specialised financial literacy workshops for administrative officials at the 
Chief Minister’s Secretariat, Meghalaya, and organised sessions at the Governor's Houses of 
Uttarakhand and Meghalaya to strengthen financial awareness among government functionaries. 


278 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SEc.4] 


e Financial Inclusion at the Grassroots Level 

To promote financial inclusion, the Directorate organised dedicated financial literacy programmes for 
Women Self-Help Groups across Meghalaya, Mizoram, Tripura, Arunachal Pradesh, Sikkim, and the 
Andaman & Nicobar Islands in collaboration with the respective State Rural Development Departments 
and Rural Livelihood Missions, reaching over ,000 SHGs. 


e Citizen Engagement through MyGov Platform 

The Directorate leveraged the Government of India’s MyGov platform to deepen citizen engagement, 
reaching over 46,950 individuals through quizzes, slogan-writing, poster-making, reel-making, and 
poetry competitions on financial literacy themes. 


e Nationwide Outreach Campaign 

The Directorate launched the nationwide 'Vittiyagyan — ICAI Ka Abhiyan’, comprising financial 
literacy workshops and practical sessions organised through ICAI Regional Councils and Branches, along 
with a multilingual radio awareness campaign in 0 Indian languages that reached over 3.93 crore 
listeners by simplifying complex financial concepts for the general public. 


e Financial Literacy Week 2025 

Financial Literacy Week was observed from 7—23 August 2025 with the support of ICAI's five Regional 
Councils and 85 Branches, during which awareness programmes were organised across the country, 
reaching over 3,000 participants. 


(II) Major Achievements 


World Record Recognition 

The Financial and Tax Literacy Directorate established a world record by conducting over 90 Financial 
and Tax Literacy programmes across the country within nine months (March—November 2025), earning 
recognition from the World Book of Records, London. 


(III) Institutional Outreach Programmes 

The Directorate conducted Financial and Tax Literacy workshops at various government institutions, 
educational establishments, and uniformed services organisations, including the National Institute of 
Immunology, Indian National Science Academy, Regional Centre for Biotechnology, National Cadet 
Corps, Bharat Scouts and Guides, leading colleges, the Central Reserve Police Force, and the Central 
Bureau of Investigation, thereby further expanding the reach and impact of its financial literacy 
initiatives nationwide. 


9.7 GRIEVANCE RESOLUTION DIRECTORATE 


Grievance Resolution Directorate (GRD) (now merged with Members & Students Services Directorate) 
was a centralized department of ICAI formed to oversee the support system for our students, members 
and other stakeholders for their engagement with institute's various activities which is delivered through 
multiple departments, committees dealing in respective areas. The GRD facilitates timely and effective 
grievance redressal through multiple channels, including Call Sahayata, E-Sahayata, dedicated email 
support, and social media. The Directorate focuses on active process monitoring, timeliness, root cause 
analysis, coordination with all stakeholders ensuring transparency, trust, and overall stakeholder 
satisfaction. 


Call Sahayata 


ICAI Call Sahayata (9997599975) operates from 09:00 AM To 09:00 PM, Monday to Saturday, except 
on gazetted holidays, enabling students, members and stakeholders to reach ICAI primarily through 
inbound calls for their queries. Calls received during operational hours are routed through and 
Interactive Voice Response(IVR) system, ensuring effective inbound calls management. Queries 
received outside operational hours are captured through the IVR and are addressed during working 
hours through callbacks. The helpline handles both general information and service-related queries in 
an efficient and systematic manner. 


[भाग ता---खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 279 


Multilingual Support, Operational KPIs and Statistical Analysis 


Call Sahayata continued to provide stakeholder support in Hindi, English and nine regional languages, 
ensuring wider accessibility across India. The language-wise call handling pattern reflected Hindi as the 
major medium of communication at 53.85%, followed by English at 33.2i% and regional languages at 
2.94%. Regional language support was available in Tamil, Telugu, Marathi, Kannada, Assamese, 
Kashmiri, Punjabi, Odia and Bengali, enabling stakeholders from different parts of the country to seek 
assistance in their preferred language and improving the overall inclusiveness of the grievance redressal 
mechanism. 


During the year, Call Sahayata performance was monitored through defined operational KPIs to ensure 
service quality, responsiveness and effective stakeholder support. The facility handled a total call 
volume of over i0.34 lakh calls with support from 84 agents. Of the total call activity, 76.86% related 
to inbound calls and 23.4% to outbound calls. The average talk time for inbound calls was 4.70 
minutes, with more than 2,500 calls handled per day. Peak call traffic was generally observed between 
0:00 AM and 5:00 PM. Around 82% of calls were from students, followed by members and other 
stakeholders. The highest inbound call volume was recorded on 7th July 2025, coinciding with the CA 
course result announcement, while the highest outbound call activity was recorded on 3rd April 2025. 


E-Sahayata (e-Tickets) 


It is an online grievance redressal platform accessible at https://eservices.icai.org/through which 
students and members can raise tickets directly. In cases where stakeholders are not satisfied with the 
support received or where resolution is delayed, tickets may also be raised through Call Sahayata agents 
on their behalf. It functions based on ICAI activities and the officers responsible for those activities, 
referred to as the escalation matrix, which is critical for effective grievance resolution 


Performance Highlights Summary 


E-Sahayata continued to serve as an effective online grievance redressal platform for students and 
members, ensuring timely support and structured escalation through the ICAI activity-based escalation 
matrix. During February 2025 to January 2026, the platform handled 30,874 grievances, out of which 
30,79 tickets were successfully closed, achieving an impressive 99% resolution rate. The platform also 
demonstrated strong operational efficiency, with 85.5% of tickets resolved within one week, reflecting 
prompt response, effective coordination, and commitment to stakeholder satisfaction. 


Comprehensive Training for Enhanced Grievance Support (2025-2026) 


During 2025-2026, the Grievance Redressal Department (GRD) conducted 2 comprehensive training 
sessions for the Call Sahayata Team to enhance grievance handling. Designed using trend analysis and 
recurring queries, these sessions involved close collaboration with key departments, including CA Exam, 
Articleship, UDIN, CPE, SSP, Campus Placement, BOS Scholarship, PDC, and Member Training. 
Additionally, GRD proactively coordinated with these departments to update FAQs, ensuring 
stakeholders always receive the most accurate and up-to-date information. 


Key Service Enhancements and Digital Initiatives 


e Rationalization of Call Categories: Call categories (dispositions and sub-dispositions) were 
comprehensively reviewed to improve relevance, simplify call classification, and generate 
meaningful insights. The categories were rationalized from over 70 sub-disposition options to 
7 primary dispositions (Student, Examination, Articles, CDS, Member, Firm, and Committee) 
with 85 streamlined sub-dispositions, enabling more accurate categorization and efficient 
handling of stakeholder queries. 

e Enhancement of E-Sahayata System: The E-Sahayata escalation matrix was 
comprehensively reviewed to simplify activities, standardize categories, and ensure correct 
officer mapping for faster grievance resolution. The matrix was updated across Regions, 
Committees, Departments, Boards, and Directorates, with query options rationalized from 377 


280 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SEc.4] 


to 22. Additionally, a self-service password reset facility, along with a user guide, was 
introduced for Department/Committee users, reducing login issues and improving grievance 
resolution timelines. 

e Optimization of Interactive Voice Response (IVR) System: The Interactive Voice 
Response (IVR) system, an automated telephony system that guides callers through voice 
prompts and keypad inputs, was optimized by simplifying the menu structure and eliminating 
redundant options to reduce call hang-ups and enable faster connectivity for stakeholders. The 
revised IVR framework was implemented. Additionally, regular follow-up with the call service 
provider reduced the average call connection time from 7-8 seconds to 4-5 seconds, further 
enhancing the stakeholder experience. 

e SMS Integration in Call Sahayata: Earlier, SMS alerts for grievance tickets generated 
through Call Sahayata were not available. During the year, SMS notifications were introduced, 
enabling stakeholders to receive instant ticket confirmations after calls and facilitating easier 
tracking of grievances, thereby enhancing transparency and communication. 

e Initiative for Updated FAQs: The Directorate proactively followed up with Departments, 
Committees, Boards, and Directorates to update and standardize FAQs, with the objective of 
providing concise, accurate, and up-to-date information through the ICAI Call Sahayata Portal, 
ensuring easier access, an improved user experience, and enhanced self-service for 
stakeholders. 


9.8 HUMAN RESOURCES DIRECTORATE 


The Human Resources Directorate of ICAI (now Human Resource Department) focused on HR-related 
matters across all offices of ICAI, such as recruitment, transfer and rotation, performance management, 
and training and development. HRD continued to strengthen the Institute’s talent acquisition efforts 
during the year, wherein recruitment for middle and senior-level positions was carried out through 
advertisements and interviews. 43 new employees joined various branches across India under the 
Branch Human Resource Scheme (BHRS) 2022. With ICAI’s expanding footprint and evolving 
responsibilities, the Human Resources Department remains committed to building an agile and future- 
ready workforce through strategic HR initiatives, continuous capacity building, and a culture of 
professionalism, inclusivity, and high performance. 


The development of a Human Resource Management System (HRMS) and a mobile-based Employee 
Self-Service (ESS) application was initiated to integrate various HR processes and enable employees to 
access HR services more efficiently. Group Term Life Insurance coverage was also introduced for 
employees to provide financial support to their families in the event of any unforeseen circumstances 


The key initiatives during the year are as under: 


e Recruitment: Recruitment under the Branch Employees Human Resource Scheme, 2022 was 
undertaken to augment resources at various Branch offices. Additionally, the recruitment process 
for key positions at the Head Office was also initiated. 


e Employee Welfare: Initiatives such as insurance coverage for regular employees through a 
Group Term Life Insurance policy and a Directors’ & Officers’ Liability policy were taken to provide 
coverage. 


e HR Automation: It was decided to implement a comprehensive Human Resource Management 
System (HRMS) to enable the delivery of all HR processes on a single platform; accordingly, the 
automation of HR data through the HRMS package has been initiated. 


9.9 LEGAL DIRECTORATE 


Legal Directorate is responsible for the enforcement and compliance with the provisions of the 
Chartered Accountants Act, 949 and Rules & Regulations made thereunder. It takes necessary action 
to protect and defend the interest of the Institute in the cases filed against it, provides legal opinions 
to various Departments within ICAI on adherence to the statutory mandate, defends regulatory actions 


[भाग एा---खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 28 


in various courts, collaborates with other Departments to enforce regulations and penalize violations as 
per the statutory mandate. 


The significant legal developments concerning the Institute and the profession during the year are as 
under: 


(I) Supreme Court Rules in Favour of Chartered Accountants — Parity with Advocates for 
Appointment as Member (Accountant), ITAT 


The Hon’ble Supreme Court restored parity between Chartered Accountants and Advocates in the 
matter of the minimum experience criteria for appointment as Members of the Income Tax Appellate 
Tribunal (ITAT). In a landmark judgement pronounced on 20th November 2025, a Bench of the Hon’ble 
Supreme Court comprising the Chief Justice of India and Justice K. Vinod Chandran invalidated the rule 
requiring 25 years of experience for the post of Member (Accountant) of the ITAT. The ruling places 
Chartered Accountants on a par with Advocates regarding the minimum experience criteria and sets 
right the injustice to Chartered Accountants under the Rules, which prescribed a minimum experience 
of 25 years for a Chartered Accountant to be considered for the post of Member (Accountant) as against 
only i0 years for Advocates to be considered for the post of Member (Judicial). 


Historically, the minimum experience requirements for both Chartered Accountants and Advocates were 
the same, before the new Rules disturbed this parity to the disadvantage of Chartered Accountants. 


The judgement restores the status quo between Advocates and Chartered Accountants in the matter 
of the minimum experience requirement, which had occupied the field for many years. 


(II) Directions of the Hon’ble Supreme Court in Himanshu Singh vs. UOI (the “Supertech 
Case”) 


The Hon’ble Supreme Court of India, on 29th April 2025, in the case of Himanshu Singh vs. Union of 
India (popularly known as the Supertech Case), issued various directions to the Government of India, 
the Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs, RERA, the RBI, the CBI and ICAI. More than 70 petitions 
were filed by over ,200 home buyers in the Supreme Court alleging the systematic failure of statutory 
and government authorities to discharge their statutory functions, the circumvention of the regulatory 
framework by banks and housing finance corporations, and the resultant illicit benefits said to have 
been drawn by builders/developers, with home buyers bearing the brunt of such failures. 


In so far as ICAI is concerned, the Hon’ble Court directed as follows: 


79, The President, Institute of Chartered Accountants of India is directed to deploy three Chartered 
Accountants, who are well conversant with the process of forensic audit and place them at the disposal 
of CBI to assist the Bureau/SIT in the matter of subject enquiry/investigations. The details of the 
remuneration to be paid to these Chartered Accountants be forwarded to this Court so that an 
appropriate order in that regard can be passed on the next date of hearing.” 


9.40 MEMBERS & STUDENTS SERVICES DIRECTORATE (M&SS) 


The Members & Students Services (M&SS) Directorate is one of the key functional Directorates of the 
Institute entrusted with administering the regulatory, operational and service-related functions 
concerning members, students and firms in accordance with the provisions of the Chartered 
Accountants Act, 949, the Chartered Accountants Regulations, 988, and the Guidelines, decisions 
and directions issued by the Council, the Executive Committee and the Management Committee from 
time to time. 


The Directorate serves as the principal interface for delivering a wide range of services relating to 
enrolment, admission to fellowship, grant and restoration of Certificate of Practice, registration and 
regulation of firms, practical and industrial training, student registration and allied services, 


282 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SECc.4] 


maintenance of statutory records and registers, issuance of various certificates and identity cards, 
organisation of convocations, publication of statutory notifications, and grievance redressal. 


With the Institute's growing membership and student base and the increasing emphasis on technology- 
enabled governance, the Directorate continues to strengthen service delivery by simplifying procedures, 
ensuring effective implementation of regulatory policies, enhancing digital services through the Self- 
Service Portal (SSP), promoting paperless processes, developing efficient monitoring and management 
information systems, and improving coordination across the Head Office and Regional Offices. 


The Directorate also operates a dedicated grievance redressal mechanism through E-Sahaayataa and 
Call Sahaayataa, ensuring timely, transparent and responsive resolution of concerns raised by members, 
students and firms. Through these initiatives, the Directorate remains committed to providing efficient, 
accountable and stakeholder-centric services while supporting the Institute's objective of excellence in 
professional administration.

9.l PUBLICATION DIRECTORATE 


The Publication Directorate (erstwhile Publication & CDS Directorate) plays a pivotal role in ensuring 
the availability of ICAI’s educational and professional publications to students, members, and other 
stakeholders. The Directorate oversees the printing, inventory management, distribution, and 
accounting of study materials, member publications, and other official publications of the Institute. The 
Directorate manages the Institute’s online Publication Portal (https://cds.icai.org/), which enables 
stakeholders to purchase publications and artefacts through a seamless digital platform. The portal 
supports online ordering, payment processing, and delivery through the Centralized Distribution System 
(CDS), thereby enhancing accessibility and service efficiency. 


To maintain an efficient supply chain, the Directorate administers inventory across designated storage 
locations and undertakes periodic physical stock verification. Stock valuation and inventory records are 
maintained in coordination with the Accounts Department to support accurate financial reporting and 
effective inventory control. The Directorate also undertakes reconciliation of publication sales with 
payment gateway collections and facilitates the integration of sales and dispatch information with the 
Institute’s ERP system. This ensures transparency, accuracy, and timely accounting of publication- 
related transactions. Through these functions, the Publication Directorate contributes significantly to 
the Institute’s objective of providing timely access to quality educational resources while maintaining 
robust inventory management, financial discipline, and operational transparency. 


(I) New Publications Brought Out 
During the period from ist April 2025 to 3ist March 2026, the Publication Directorate printed various 


new publications on behalf of the Board of Studies (Academic) and other Committees, which are also 
hosted on the Publication Portal. 


286 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SEc.4] 


(II) Centralised Distribution System (CDS) 


Since July 207, all publications of ICAI, including study materials, Revision Test Papers, member- 
related publications and various mementoes such as ties, tie pins, cuff links and lapel pins, are 
dispatched centrally through the CDS in the Publication Portal (https://cds.icai.org/) to students, 
members and other stakeholders based on orders received through the portal.

(IV) Future Endeavours 


The Directorate is at the final stage of developing a new Publication Portal. The new portal is expected 
to go live shortly and will provide an enhanced user experience, improved operational efficiency, better 
monitoring of publication orders, and reduced turnaround time for processing and delivery to students, 
members and other stakeholders. 


9.[2 REGION & BRANCH AFFAIRS (RBA) DIRECTORATE 


As ICAI continues to strengthen its presence and serve a vibrant community of over 4 lakh members 
and more than 9 lakh students, the Region & Branch Affairs (RBA) Directorate plays a vital role in 
ensuring the seamless and effective functioning of the Institute’s extensive pan-India network of 5 
Regional Councils and 86 Branches. Acting as the central coordinating bridge between the 
Headquarters and regional formations, the Directorate provides strategic guidance, administrative 
support, and operational oversight to foster consistency, efficiency, and excellence across the 
organization. 


Through enhanced institutional coordination, process optimization, knowledge dissemination, and 
capacity-building initiatives, the Directorate enables regional and branch-level units to effectively deliver 
ICAI’s educational, professional, and member-focused programmes. By promoting collaboration, 
strengthening stakeholder engagement, and supporting continuous improvement, the RBA Directorate 
significantly contributes to advancing ICAI’s mission of upholding excellence, integrity, professionalism, 
and public trust, while embracing technology, transformation, and innovation in the service of the 
profession and nation-building. 


(I) Regional Councils and their Branches 


The Institute has five Regional Councils — Western India Regional Council (WIRC), Southern India 
Regional Council (SIRC), Eastern India Regional Council (EIRC), Central India Regional Council (CIRC) 
and Northern India Regional Council (NIRC) — with their Headquarters at Mumbai, Chennai, Kolkata, 
Kanpur and New Delhi respectively. The total number of Branches of Regional Councils is 86. With a 
view to actively involving students of the CA course in developing a spirit of fellowship and promoting 
social, cultural, academic and intellectual development, the Council has encouraged the setting up of 
Branches of Chartered Accountants Students’ Association (CASA). So far, 45 Branches of CASA have 
been set up.

9.3 SPECIFIC FUND MANAGEMENT DIRECTORATE 


The Specific Fund Management Directorate was constituted by the Institute of Chartered Accountants 
of India (ICAI) to ensure the dedicated, transparent, and efficient administration of funds established 
for social welfare, educational advancement, and charitable purposes. The Directorate is responsible 
for the effective management, continuous monitoring, and transparent reporting of these funds, 
ensuring that their resources are deployed strictly in accordance with the objectives for which they 
have been constituted. Through a robust governance framework, the Directorate seeks to maximize 
the social and professional impact of these funds while upholding the principles of accountability, 
integrity, and good governance. 


In line with ICAI's enduring commitment to the welfare of its members, students, and the larger society, 
the Directorate administers the Chartered Accountants Benevolent Fund (CABF), Chartered Accountants 
Students Benevolent Fund (CASBF) and 5. Vaidyanath Aiyar Memorial Fund (SVAMF). By 
institutionalizing a comprehensive framework for fund governance, financial management, monitoring, 
compliance, and reporting, the Specific Fund Management Directorate reinforces ICAI's core values of 
compassion, integrity, transparency, and trustworthiness 


Chartered Accountants’ Benevolent Fund 


The Chartered Accountants Benevolent Fund (CABF) has been established to provide financial 
assistance for maintenance, education, medical support, and other similar purposes to necessitous 
persons, including members or former members of the Institute irrespective of whether they were 
subscribers to the Fund, the wives and children of such members, widows and children of deceased 
members, and relatives or other dependants who relied upon a deceased member for support where 
the member has passed away without leaving behind a widow or child. Through timely financial 
assistance, the CABF serves as a vital social security mechanism, extending support to members and 
their families during periods of financial hardship and reinforcing the profession's spirit of solidarity and 
mutual care.

S. Vaidyanath Aiyar Memorial Fund 


The Directorate is further entrusted with the administration of the S. Vaidyanath Aiyar Memorial Fund 
(SVAMF), established to commemorate the memory and enduring contributions of Late CA S. 
Vaidyanath Aiyar, a former President of ICAI. The Fund has been created through voluntary 
contributions from members of the Institute with the objective of recognizing professional excellence, 
promoting professional academic through lectures and research activities, and supporting deserving 
students. 

The number of life membership of the Fund as on 3lst March 2026 is ii,089. The balance in the 
Corpus Fund was Rs. 8i,85,i50/- as on 3ist March 2026 as against Rs. 8i,25,i50/- as on 3ist March, 
2025. 


Chartered Accountants Student’s Benevolent Fund (CASBF) 


The Directorate also manages the Chartered Accountants Students Benevolent Fund (CASBF), which 
has been established to provide financial assistance to economically weaker Chartered Accountancy 
students, enabling them to pursue their professional education without undue financial constraints. The 
Fund provides medical financial assistance to deserving students facing medical emergencies, 
reimburses course and examination fees of economically weaker students whose parent or guardian is 
receiving assistance under the Chartered Accountants Benevolent Fund, and grant scholarships to 
financially distressed students who have lost both parents. Through these initiatives, the CASBF seeks 
to ensure that financial adversity does not become an impediment to pursuing the Chartered 
Accountancy course and supports deserving students in successfully completing their professional 
education. 


The Fund was established in August, 2008 with the aim and objective to provide financial assistance to 
the students registered with ICAI. The balance in the credit of the general fund was Rs.6,37,29,559/- 
as on 3i% March, 2026 as against Rs. 7,3,64,48/- as on 3% March, 2025. 


290 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SEc.4] 


9.4 TENDER MONITORING DIRECTORATE 


In order to monitor, analyze and streamline tendering for professional services of Chartered 
Accountants, a Directorate namely "Tender Monitoring Directorate" was constituted by the Council 
during the year 2023-24 which has now merged with PDC. The Directorate has been endeavouring to 
streamline the process of tendering and to monitor the bids quoted by CAs in all the permissible tenders 
and analyse them. The Directorate further refers the deviations at appropriate level. 


Major activities of the Directorate 


The Directorate has its own Portal for tenders on professional services — www.tmdicai.org. 
which offers the following features: 


Tender opportunities 

Latest information for members 

Council Guidelines on tenders 

Details of minimum recommended scale of fees 

Format of cost sheet to be submitted by members 

Extract from relevant provisions of Code of Ethics 

Representations made by Directorate to the tenderers 

Answers to Frequently Asked Questions 

Grievance posting mechanism, with or without disclosing identity. 

Fee calculator for the ease of calculating fee for audit and other assignments. 


LNNNLNLNA NARS 


(I) Representations to Tendering Organisations 


Representations are being made to tenderers where minimum fees is not prescribed in respect of audit 
& attestation services, or where eligibility conditions appear to be unreasonably restrictive or where the 
services tendered create “independence” issues. During the functional year 2025-26, 47 such 
representations were sent to the tendering organisations. 


(II) Review and Hosting of Tenders 


TMD also collects tenders for professional services from all over India and hosts the same in its TMD 
Portal. During the functional year 2025-26, 2,i2 tenders were studied, out of which ,937 tenders 
were hosted in the TMD portal as these were found appropriate in all respects. 


(III) Examination of Bids Submitted by Members 


Bids submitted by members are studied and cost sheets are called for wherever it is felt that the bid is 
for low amount. During the functional year 2025-26, notices were sent to 209 CA firms in respect of 38 
tenders. During the year 2025-26, cases of i02 CA firms were referred to Director (Discipline) for 
investigation. Peer review has been ordered in respect of 0 CA firms. Cases relating to 7 CA firms 
have been closed as the cost sheet was found to be satisfactory. Examination of rest of the cases is 
on. 


9.5 UDIN DIRECTORATE 


The Unique Document Identification Number (UDIN), introduced by ICAI, continues to play a pivotal 
role in ensuring the authenticity, reliability and transparency of documents certified by Chartered 
Accountants. By assigning a system-generated unique identification number to every certificate, audit 
report and attestation, UDIN serves as a robust mechanism to prevent document forgery and misuse. 
Over the years, UDIN has evolved into a critical component of the regulatory ecosystem, facilitating 
seamless verification of documents by regulators, government authorities, financial institutions and 
other users. During the year under review, UDIN witnessed significant advancements in technological 
transformation, regulatory integration and functional enhancements. 


[भाग त-खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 


29] 


(I) UDIN as a Decision-Making Parameter for Regulators, Banks and Other Stakeholders 


MoU with the Reserve Bank of India: ICAI signed an MoU with the RBI on 6th February 2026 
at ICAI Bhawan, New Delhi, during the 448th Council Meeting, to facilitate real-time verification 
of UDINs through secure API integration. The initiative aligns with RBI’s end-to-end digital 
transformation framework and reflects ICAI’s commitment to technology-driven governance. 
MoU with the Indian Army (DGST): An MoU was signed on 6th November 2025 with the 
Indian Army, Directorate General of Supplies and Transport, for integration of UDIN verification 
within the vendor registration process of the Army Purchase Organisation (APO), enabling 
automated authentication of CA-certified documents. The MoU was executed in the presence of 
Lieutenant General Mukesh Chadha, AVSM, SM, VSM, Director General of Supplies and Transport. 
Ministry of Home Affairs (Project FCRA 2.0): The Ministry of Home Affairs approached the 
UDIN Directorate for API-based integration to enable real-time verification of certificates and 
audit reports submitted under the Foreign Contribution (Regulation) Act, 20i0. A meeting was 
held on 2ist April 2026 at the FCRA Wing, New Delhi, to deliberate on the requirements for 
enabling API-based UDIN verification. 

Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology (MeitY): Continued engagement for 
integration of UDIN with the Entity Locker platform, a secure digital repository for document 
storage and sharing. Two meetings were held in 2025 (47th March and 28th July) to discuss API- 
based integration requirements. 

C&AG Empanelment: The C&AG used UDIN data as one of the elements for empanelment of 
CA firms/LLPs for the first time in 2023-24. For 2026-27, points are awarded based on UDINs 
generated during 2022-23, 2023-24 and 2024-25, with the window to be scaled to 5 years by 
2026-27. 


(II) Technology Transformation and Digital Initiatives 


Migration to DigiCA Platform: The UDIN Portal was successfully migrated to the DigiCA 
Platform on 20th December 2025, providing an enhanced user interface, improved system 
performance and advanced functionalities. Post-migration, the portal witnessed substantial 
improvements in system stability, responsiveness and user experience, with better load handling, 
improved uptime and enhanced data security. 


Mobile Application (iOS and Android): A mobile application was launched in a phased 
manner, enabling members to generate, view, download and revoke UDINs in real time, 
enhancing accessibility and operational efficiency during urgent and time-sensitive professional 
engagements. 


Security Audit by C-DAC: The Vulnerability Assessment and Penetration Testing 
(VAPT)/Security Audit of the UDIN web portal under the DigiCA Platform was entrusted to C- 
DAC. The Security Audit Certificate for the web portal was received on 7th December 2025, and 
the Security Audit Certificate for the iOS Mobile Application was received on i9th January 2026. 


(III) Functional Enhancements for Members 


PAN Validation for GST & Tax Audit UDINs: Pursuant to the 447th Council Meeting decision, 
the PAN of the assessee has been introduced as a mandatory additional field during UDIN 
generation under the GST & Tax Audit category for validation at the e-Filing Portal, applicable for 
UDINs generated from 20th December 2025 onwards. 

Preceding Year Audit Details: Pursuant to the 442nd Council Meeting decision, a provision 
has been enabled on the UDIN Portal to capture preceding year audit details during UDIN 
generation under the GST & Tax Audit and Audit & Assurance Function categories, made 
operational with effect from 20th December 2025. 

Ceiling on Tax Audit UDINs: Pursuant to the 442nd Council Meeting decision, a provision 
introducing a ceiling on the maximum number of UDINs that may be generated under Tax Audit 
assignments, in alignment with the prescribed limit of 60 tax audits, is under development. 


292 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY 


[PART III—SEC.4] 


Counting of Tax Audit assignments has been made effective from ist April 2026. Field-Level 
Validation was implemented on iith February 2026 for all sub-categories under Section 44AB. 

e Auditor’s Opinion Functionality: Pursuant to the 446th Council Meeting decision, the 
functionality to capture “Auditor’s Opinion” during UDIN generation was implemented — for Audit 
& Assurance Functions from midnight of 20th June 2025, and for GST & Tax Audit from midnight 


of 28th June 2025. 


e Inclusion of New Forms: Form 26AANX was enabled on the UDIN Portal on 22nd October 
2025 on the request of CBDT; Form 46 under the Income-tax Act, 2025 was incorporated from 


ist April 2026.

9.6 ICAI PLACEMENT DIRECTORATE 


The ICAI Placement Directorate of The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India was formed in the 
year 2026 with the objective of creating a dedicated and focused platform for facilitating career 
opportunities for Chartered Accountants and strengthening industry-profession engagement. The 
Directorate acts as a bridge between industry and the Chartered Accountancy profession by providing 
structured, transparent, and technology-driven placement and career support services for Newly 
Qualified Chartered Accountants (NQCAs), Experienced Chartered Accountants, semi-qualified 
professionals, and organizations across diverse sectors and geographies. 


With a vision to empower finance professionals and support organizations in accessing quality talent, 
the ICAI Placement Directorate undertakes various initiatives including Campus Placement 
Programmes, ICAI Campus Placement+, Career Ascent Programmes, HR Meets, Overseas Placement 
Initiatives, CA Career Catalyst Programmes, Orientation Programmes, and Management Development 
Programmes (MDPs) for CA Rank Holders. The Directorate also offers continuous recruitment and 
networking support through its digital platforms, enabling organizations to connect with highly skilled 
finance professionals throughout the year while helping Chartered Accountants explore opportunities 
across domains 


The major initiatives are as under: 
(I) CA Career Catalyst Programme 2026 


The Directorate launched the CA Career Catalyst Programme 2026, a comprehensive virtual career- 
readiness initiative designed for newly qualified Chartered Accountants and CA Final qualified 
candidates, conducted from i5th to 22nd March 2026 in a phased manner. During the period from i5th 
to 20th March 2026, domain-focused webinar sessions were organised daily covering important sectors 
and emerging career opportunities for Chartered Accountants, including taxation, mergers and 
acquisitions, finance, investment banking, statutory audit, internal audit, financial due diligence, 
forensic accounting, consultancy, advisory, FP&A, costing, budgeting and offshore opportunities. 


Further, on 2ist and 22nd March 2026, structured sessions on Group Discussion, HR Training, Mock 
Interviews and One-to-One Mentoring were organised in batches to equip participants with placement 
readiness, sector-selection guidance and interview preparedness. The programme received an 
encouraging response and served as a structured bridge between the declaration of the CA Final results 
and the commencement of placement activities. 


294 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SEc.4] 


(II) 6470 ICAI Campus Placement Programme 


The 64th ICAI Campus Placement Programme was organised across 29 centres (9 major and 20 smaller 
centres) during April-May 2026 for Chartered Accountants who qualified in the January 2026 CA Final 
Examination, marking the third campus placement opportunity within a year for recently qualified 
members. The programme witnessed the registration of 5,834 candidates (out of 7,592 newly qualified 
CAs) and participation of over 36 organisations, offering 9,000+ vacancies, reflecting continued trust 
in ICAI’s Campus Placement Programme and strong employer confidence. 


Participation from a wide spectrum of reputed organisations, including large MNCs, technology 
companies, consulting firms, professional services networks, Global Capability Centres, start-ups and 
leading Indian corporates, further underscored the industry's confidence in ICAI's talent pool. The 
campus cycle also recorded robust demand across both traditional and emerging domains, including 
finance, audit, consulting, data analytics, AI workflow automation, business intelligence, investment 
banking, private equity, risk management, compliance and digital transformation. 


The programme witnessed strong hiring demand despite global geopolitical uncertainties, restructuring 
activity and layoffs reported in certain sectors. Annual CTCs offered ranged from %9 lakh to %27.50 
lakh, and a standardised orientation programme was conducted across all centres to familiarise 
candidates with the placement process, career opportunities, employer expectations and interview 
readiness. 


(III) CA Jobs Portal 


In addition to campus placement initiatives, the Directorate continued to strengthen the CA Jobs Portal 
(https://cajobs.icai.org/), which provides 24x7, 365-day employment opportunities for Chartered 
Accountants across sectors and experience levels. Through the portal, the Institute facilitates hiring 
through virtual interviews and immediate vacancy fulfilment at various levels, offering opportunities for 
senior-level CA positions, women-focused placements, work-from-home opportunities, lateral 
placements for experienced Chartered Accountants and Director-level opportunities. Employers with 
urgent hiring requirements can directly source qualified CA talent through the portal or write to 
icai.placement@icai.in for assistance and support. 


(IV) Management Development Programme for CA Rank Holders 


The Directorate conducted the Management Development Programme for CA Rank Holders of the 
January 2026 batch in collaboration with the Indian Institute of Management Kozhikode. The 
programme was designed to provide structured management exposure to rank holders in areas such 
as strategic thinking, leadership, communication, AI for finance professionals, governance, decision- 
making and emerging business leadership perspectives. 


Out of 57 eligible rank holders, 33 participated in the programme, reflecting a highly encouraging 
response; considering the number of participants and residential logistics, the programme was 
organised in two separate batches to ensure effective delivery, focused learning and smooth participant 
management. The programme received highly encouraging feedback from both the participating rank 
holders, who appreciated the quality of academic inputs, practical exposure, peer learning and the 
leadership-oriented design, and the Management Institute, which acknowledged the enthusiasm, 
discipline and active engagement of the CA rank holders throughout the sessions.

i0.l BOARD OF STUDIES — ACADEMICS 

The Board of Studies- Academics, a non-standing Committee of ICAI, formulates and implements 
policies and programmes relating to education and training of students. The mission of the Board of 


Studies is to provide a strong foundation of professional knowledge, skills and values, ethics and 
attitudes that enable students of chartered accountancy course to evolve as competent professionals 


[भाग ता---खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 295 


and adapt to changes throughout their professional career. The Board of Studies (Academic) 
significantly contributes to the academic and professional growth of aspiring CA students by offering 
comprehensive educational support, continuous guidance and mentorship throughout their journey 
towards becoming Chartered Accountants. 


To strengthen students’ professional knowledge and values, the BoS (A) offers quality Study Materials, 
Revision Test Papers, Mock Test Papers, Suggested Answers, Saransh — Last Mile Referencer and Case 
Scenario Booklets for all subjects of the CA course. It also promotes effective learning through digital 
initiatives such as Live Virtual Classes, Live Virtual Revisionary Classes, the ICAI Digital Learning Campus 
and the MCQ Paper Practice Assessment Dashboard.

(I) Student-Centred Learning Resources

0.2. BOARD OF STUDIES — OPERATIONS 


The Board of Studies — Operations, a non-standing committee of the Institute, is continuously focusing 
on providing world-class training to Chartered Accountancy students. It conducts regulatory training 
programmes for Chartered Accountancy students, including Orientation, Information Technology, 
Advanced Information Technology, and Management & Communication Skills courses through its IT 
and Soft Skills Centres across India, as well as four-week residential programmes at the Centres of 
Excellence in Hyderabad and Jaipur. 


The Board also organises various student-focused events, including International, National, State and 
Branch-level conferences, seminars, workshops, sports competitions, festivals, counselling 
programmes, industrial visits, talent contests and provides scholarships to CA students. These initiatives 
are aimed at equipping students with professional competencies, managerial, communication, 
presentation, and soft skills to meet the evolving requirements of the accountancy profession and the 
changing business and regulatory environment.

0.3 COMMITTEE ON CAREER COUNSELLING 


The Career Counselling Committee of ICAI, constituted in 20i5, serves as the outreach arm of the 
Institute, dedicated to creating awareness among students and parents about Chartered Accountancy 
and career opportunities in the commerce domain. In alignment with the national vision of a Viksit 
Bharat and sustained economic development, the Committee recognizes that a strong foundation in 
commerce education is essential to build a financially literate, ethical, and competent workforce. During 
2025-26, the Committee intensified its efforts to reach students in schools, colleges, and Tier-2/Tier-3 
cities through career seminars, mentorship drives, and digital outreach. By equipping students with 
information on academic pathways, skill requirements, and career prospects, the Committee is 
committed to building a robust pipeline of future professionals who will contribute to nation-building 
and the vision of Viksit Bharat. 


Key activities 


Project Vanijya - The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI), launched “Project Vanijya - 
Shaping the Future of Students for Bharat as a Developed Country” on 9"° December 2025 at New 
Delhi. The initiative aims to strengthen commerce education from the school level in line with the 
Hon’ble Prime Minister’s vision of Viksit Bharat @2047. The Representatives of Education Boards from 
5 States across India were presented. The programme focuses on guiding students towards commerce 
education and professional opportunities, contributing to India’s journey towards becoming a developed 
nation. 


Exposure Visits of CBSE School Principals- The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India signed 
an MoU with CBSE to promote commerce education and career awareness among students. As part of 
this initiative, the Committee on Career Counselling organised Exposure Visits for CBSE School Principals 
at the Centres of Excellence in Jaipur and Hyderabad, as well as at Mumbai, Indore, and Guwahati.

These visits familiarised principals with the Chartered Accountancy (CA) Course, new education scheme, 
and diverse career opportunities in the profession. The initiative received an encouraging response, 
with principals expressing keen interest in conducting career counselling programmes in their schools. 
Some participants and their family members also enrolled for the CA Course, reflecting the growing 
impact of the initiative. 


Faculty Development Programmes (FDP)- Committee organised a series of Faculty Development

Programmes benefiting approximately 6,3,i79 students across the country. Additionally, the 
Committee participated in 24 career/education fairs across India, reaching more than i6,840 students.

provided comprehensive career guidance in the field of Commerce, empowering students to make 
informed decisions and choose the right career path for their future.

.l XBRLINDIA 


For the effective participation of regulators, technologists, corporates and academicians in the 
Institute’s endeavour to promote XBRL in India, the Institute facilitated the incorporation of XBRL India 
as a Section 8 Company (formerly Section 25 under the Companies Act, 956), with the main objective 
of promoting and encouraging the adoption of XBRL as the standard for electronic business reporting 
in India, through the development of taxonomies and facilitation of education and training. XBRL India 
is an established Indian jurisdiction of XBRL International. 


(I) Development and Maintenance of Taxonomies for MCA 


XBRL India is currently developing and maintaining XBRL taxonomies for the Ministry of Corporate 
Affairs (MCA). Two Taxonomies are presently applicable to companies: the Ind AS Taxonomy and the 
Commercial and Industrial (C&I) Taxonomy. XBRL India submitted samples of Instance Documents for 
testing purposes of taxonomies to MCA (initially submitted in 2022), and meetings and discussions were 
held during the year with MCA officials regarding the updation of taxonomies. Queries and issues faced 
by filers with regard to taxonomies while converting Financial Statements to XBRL formats have also 
been resolved.

taxonomies for the following sectors (currently under review by MCA): Non-Banking Finance Companies 
(NBFCs); Insurance Entities (both life and non-life insurance companies, separately). 


(III) Participation in International Activities 


As a member of XBRL International, the contribution of XBRL India extends beyond India. Board 
members of XBRL India regularly represent India at various meetings and events of XBRL International.

.2 ICAI- Accounting Research Foundation (ARF) 


ICAI Accounting Research Foundation (ICAI ARF) was established in January, 999 to be developed as 
an academy for imparting, spreading and promoting knowledge, learning, education and understanding 
in the various fields related to the profession of accountancy. The following is the detail of projects 
undertaken by ICAI ARF: 


e Indian Railways: Indian Railways, the fourth largest railway network in the world after the 

United States, Russia and China, is undertaking several initiatives towards Accounting Reforms 
in line with generally accepted accounting principles. In its journey of accounting reforms, the 
Indian Railways had initiated exercise of preparation of its Accrual Based Financial Statements 
in 20i4, marking a major shift towards enhanced financial transparency and accountability. 
Since the inception of this initiative, the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) and 
its Accounting Research Foundation (ICAI ARF) have played a pivotal role in supporting these 
reforms. 
ICAI ARF has been instrumental in guiding Indian Railways through the migration to an accrual- 
based accounting system. ICAI ARF have successfully prepared the Accrual Based Financial 
Statements for all Zonal Railways and Production Units from the financial year 20i5-6 to 2022- 
23 under the grafting method. 


Initiatives during the year 

l. For the first time, Accrual Based Financial Statements (ABFS) of Indian Railways under 
transactional method for the financial year 2023-24 prepared and submitted. Several 
initiatives towards environment Sustainability undertaken by the Indian Railways have been 
reflected in the financial statements. ABFS for the year 2023-24 under grafting method is 
also under development for comparison with new transactional method. 


2. ABFS of Indian Railways for the year 2024-25 under transactional method is under progress. 


3. Proposal to review Accounts/Finance Codes, relevance of various account heads and 
allocation/classification of expenditure and earnings on Indian Railways (RAFC) has been 
initiated and is currently under progress. 


e Treasuries and Accounts Department, Government of Tamil Nadu: ICAI ARF has 
undertaken the task of Re-writing of Tamil Nadu Financial Code, Volume I & II. The ICAI has 
submitted the Report during the year to the department. 


e Impact Study of GST reforms 2.0 on Scheme of Budgetary Support under Goods and 
Services Tax Regime - The Department for Promotion of Industry and Internal Trade (DPIIT) 
awarded the project to ICAI ARF for undertaking the study of GST reforms 2.0 on Scheme of 
Budgetary Support under Goods and Services Tax Regime. The project is under progress.

held till date.

i.4 INDIAN INSTITUTE OF INSOLVENCY PROFESSIONALS OF ICAI (IIIPI) 


The Indian Institute of Insolvency professionals of ICAI ‘IIIPI’, a wholly owned subsidiary of the 
Institute of Chartered Accountants of India ‘ICAI’, is a section 8 Company promoted by ICAI to enrol 
and regulate insolvency professionals as its members in accordance with the Insolvency and Bankruptcy 
code 206 and read with regulations. IIIPI has been awarded with the registration certificate as the 
First Insolvency Professional Agency of India by Hon'ble Union Finance Minister on 287 November 206 
at Delhi. IIIPI is the largest IPA with nearly two-third of members in India, has attracted members from 
a diverse stream including Chartered Accountant, Company Secretary, Cost Accountant, Advocates and 
Management Professionals. It has networked with other national and international bodies in furtherance 
of the capacity and knowledge building of stakeholders on the one hand and providing value-added 
policy inputs for the evolving law and practice of the IBC on the other. 


IIIPI is one of the key pillarsin the insolvency and bankruptcy regime. It is 3 front-line 
regulator required to discharge multiple statutory responsibilities. It is responsible for the development 
and regulation of insolvency profession and professionals, besides building knowledge and capacity of 
stakeholders. In a parlance, it is a mini-State, last in the hierarchy of parliamentary delegation of 
authority with regulatory, executive and quasi-judicial responsibilities. 


304 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SEc.4] 


Out of the total 4,643 Insolvency Professionals registered with the IBBI as on 30" April 2026, 2,907, 
i.e. 62.64%, are from ITIPI. IIIPI’s members have so far handled approximately 75% of the overall 
assignments of CIRP/Liquidation/Voluntary Liquidation, including many of the largest cases.

the areas of real estate resolutions, the PPIRP framework, insolvency laws in India vis-a-vis 
internationally, and resolving insolvency in FPCs in East India. 


(IV) Monitoring of Insolvency Professionals 


As per the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Board of India (Model Bye-laws and Governing Board of 
Insolvency Professional Agencies) Regulations, 206, IIIPI is required to monitor the professional 
activities and conduct of its professional members for their adherence to the provisions of the Code, 
Rules, Regulations and Guidelines issued thereunder, the Bye-laws, the Code of Conduct and the 
directions given by the Governing Board. The monitoring, including inspections, is continuous and 
covers assignments undertaken by IPs, particularly Corporate Insolvency Resolution Processes (CIRP), 
Liquidation Processes, Voluntary Liquidations and individual insolvency pertaining to Personal 
Guarantors to Corporate Debtors. 


It involves a review of records, reports, filings, timelines and decision-making, to assess whether the 
IP has acted with integrity, independence and due diligence. Any deviations, lapses or non-compliances 
identified during the monitoring process are examined and appropriate actions are initiated in 
accordance with the applicable framework, which may include seeking explanations, issuing warnings, 
imposing penalties or referring the matter for disciplinary proceedings, thereby ensuring accountability 
and maintaining high standards of professional practice. 


(V) Grievance Redressal and Disciplinary Mechanism 


e During the period, IIIPI received 78 grievances and disposed of 97 grievances (including 
grievances pertaining to the previous year). Since inception, the Grievance Redressal Committee 
has disposed of 507 cases, with 32 matters remaining pending. 


e [JIPI initiated i5 disciplinary proceedings during the period and disposed of 23 matters (including 
matters pertaining to previous years). A total of 235 cases have been disposed of, while 9 matters 
remained pending. 


e Further, one appeal was received and disposed of during the period. 4 appeals have been 
disposed of since inception, with no cases pending. 


2.5 THE INSTITUTE OF SOCIAL AUDITORS OF INDIA (ISAI) 


The Institute of Social Auditors of India (ISAI) was incorporated under Section 8 of the Companies Act, 
203 on 7" October, 2022, under the aegis of ICAI, as a Self-Regulatory Organisation with the prime 
objective of being a leading institution for developing independent, ethical and world-class Social Impact 
Assessors (earlier Social Auditors) responding to the needs and expectations of stakeholders. ISAI 
enrols, regulates and develops Social Impact Assessors in an independent and transparent manner and 
also works on the capacity building of Social Impact Assessors through continuous professional 
education and training. 


306 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SEc.4] 


(I) Governing Board of ISAI 


ISAI functions through its Governing Board, which comprises a minimum of twelve Directors, namely: 
(a) a Chairperson having rich experience in the social sector/impact assessment, or from a renowned 
academic institution; (b) five Central Council Members of ICAI/the Sustainability Reporting Standards 
Board with relevant experience; (c) one person nominated by the Securities and Exchange Board of 
India (SEBI); (d) one person nominated by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA)/Ministry of Home 
Affairs (MHA)/Ministry of Social Justice & Empowerment; (6) one person nominated by the Capacity 
Building Fund; (f) one person nominated by the Social Stock Exchange; (g) one member from the 
thematic areas of the social sector; and (h) one member having cross-sectoral experience in social 
sectors. 


For the period from i* April, 2025 to 35t March, 2026, two Governing Board meetings were convened 
at New Delhi — the 0" Governing Board Meeting on 247 June, 2025 and the ii Governing Board 
Meeting on 20" September 2025. The 3 Annual General Meeting (AGM) of the Company was held on 
8' September 2025, and the Annual Return and financials have been filed with the MCA. 


(II) Sub-Committees of ISAI 


Seven Sub-Committees have been constituted in terms of the Bye-laws of the Company to regulate its 
working in the most transparent and efficient manner, comprising members of the Governing Board, 
namely the Advisory Committee, Membership Committee, Monitoring Committee, Grievance Redressal 
Committee, Appellate Committee, Disciplinary Committee and Editorial Board. During the financial year 
2025-26, two Membership Committee meetings were convened, on 6" November, 2025 and i9% 
January, 2026, through virtual/physical mode, wherein important decisions related to the allotment of 
membership of ISAI were taken. 


(III) Registration of Social Impact Assessors 


ISAI has registered 765 Social Impact Assessors, the details of which are available on the website of 
the Company. 


(IV) Online Training Course on the NISM Series XXIII: Social Impact Assessor Certification 
Examination 


ISAI conducts an online training course to facilitate preparation for the “NISM Series XXIII: Social 
Impact Assessor Certification Examination”. The online classes cover all 44 chapters of the NISM study 
material across 2i study hours with expert faculties, MCQs for each chapter, case studies and illustrative 
examples. ISAI conducted one batch of the online training course during the financial year 2025-26 
and trained 30 participants for the examination. 


(V) Webinars 


ISAI organised 5 webinars during the year for capacity building and to create awareness about the 
Social Stock Exchange, Social Audit and Social Impact Assessment amongst stakeholders and the public 
at large: 


2i.6 ICAI INTERNATIONAL ADR CENTRE (750) 


The ICAI International ADR Centre (IIAC), a Section 8 Company established by the Institute of 
Chartered Accountants of India, was formally launched on 30" July, 2025 by Hon’ble Minister of State 
(Independent Charge) for Law & Justice and Minister of State for Parliamentary Affairs, Government of 
India, at New Delhi and incorporated on 8" August, 2025. The IIAC was set up with the objective of 
promoting and institutionalising Alternative Dispute Resolution (ADR) mechanisms through arbitration, 
mediation, conciliation and other dispute resolution processes. The Centre seeks to provide efficient, 
transparent, technology-enabled and cost-effective dispute resolution services in commercial, financial, 
contractual, corporate and economic matters while upholding the principles of fairness, neutrality, 
confidentiality and procedural efficiency. 


[भाग [--खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 


307 


Major developments: 


e The IIAC marked ICAI’s entry into the institutional Alternative Dispute Resolution ecosystem 
and reaffirmed its commitment towards supporting efficient dispute resolution mechanisms and 
ease of doing business. 

e Statutory and regulatory compliances, including filings with the Ministry of Corporate Affairs, 
were undertaken for establishing the institutional framework of the Centre. 

e Drafting and finalisation of Arbitration Rules and Mediation Frameworks were initiated, drawing 
upon international best practices and aligned with the Indian legal and regulatory framework. 

e A Governing Board comprising representatives of ICAI, eminent legal professionals, former 
institutional leaders and experienced practitioners was constituted to provide strategic direction 
and strengthen the Centre's institutional governance and credibility. 

e Going forward, IIAC aims to emerge as a premier ADR institution by fostering capacity building, 
promoting awareness of ADR mechanisms, facilitating domestic and international dispute 
resolution, and contributing to the development of a robust, efficient and trusted dispute 
resolution framework. 


i2 QUALITY REVIEW BOARD (QRB) 


The Quality Review Board (QRB), established by the Central Government of India under Section 28A of 
the Chartered Accountants Act, 949 (The ‘Act’), operates as an independent body to review the quality 
of services rendered by Chartered Accountants in India. The constitution of the Board consists of a 
Chairperson, five members appointed by the Central Government, and five members nominated by the 
Council of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI), ensuring a balanced governance 
structure. 


While statutorily empowered to examine quality of all professional services under Sec. 28B of the Act, 
the QRB is presently engaged in the review of quality of statutory audit services. In accordance with 
Section 28B, its principal functions consist of: 


e To make recommendations to the Council with regard to the quality of services provided by the 
members of the Institute. 

e To review the quality of services provided by the members of the Institute including audit 
services. 

e To guide the members of the Institute to improve the quality of services and adherence to the 
various statutory and other regulatory requirements. 

e To forward cases of non-compliance with various statutory and regulatory requirements by the 
members of the Institute or firms, noticed by it during the course of its reviews, to the 
Disciplinary Directorate for its examination. 


Subsequent to the establishment of National Financial Reporting Authority (NFRA) by the Central 
Government in 20i8, the jurisdiction of QRB has been correspondingly defined. The QRB is now 
mandated to undertake reviews of audit quality in respect of entities falling outside the ambit of Rule 
3 of the NFRA Rules, 208, as well as those referred to QRB by NFRA under Rule 9(4) of NFRA Rules, 
208. 


(I) Quality Review Status of Selected Audit Firms: 


During the year 2025-26, 60 reviews of the audit quality of various audit firms were conducted by the 
Quality Review Board. The decisions taken by the Board in such cases are as under:

(II) Capacity Building — Webinar for Empanelled Technical Reviewers

and updated, and the revised SOP has been hosted on the Board's website 
(https://www.qrbca.in/). 

e The rating criteria for Technical Reviewers has been revised to ensure the selection of high-
